### [2026-06-01T03:35:24Z] architect (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from architect

Architecture analysis + 5-slice scaffold for #2908 (event-pump + durable memory). Reframe BRC consensus-agent execution from a long-lived participant that holds blocking waits into a deterministic wrapper-driven event pump that invokes the agent one-shot per actionable event, with continuity carried by a durable per-role memory file. Removes the model-driven wait re-entry seam (handlers/message.py:267,405) that produced #2906 (and the #2323 / #2064 / #2482 / #2036 / #1995 / #2451 lineage). Five-slice linear DAG with feature-flag rollout: slice-1 CLI + memory data plane (additive, no behavior change); slice-2 event-pump wrapper + liveness migration (flagged); slice-3 delta-scoped re-analysis + prompt collapse; slice-4 spike validation + flag flip + delete old capped-restart path; slice-5 MCP→CLI tool collapse. Surfaces every runtime primitive with file:line evidence (consensus_wrapper.py:38,64,116; handlers/message.py:267,405; _build_brc_preamble at routes/pipelines.py:12348; client.py:299–353 MCP registration; egg-orch CLI at orch_cli.py:132,144,324,1426,1695; action_guards.py:476–504 NACK aggregation; signals.py:1169–1201 stale-version; cost_callback.py:188 Qwen-route cache) tagged on BOTH the purpose axis (test-fixture vs deployed-pod) and execution-context axis (in-sandbox-agent vs orchestrator/gateway pod vs trusted-CI-runner) per #2594. Notable subagent-finding correction: the existing wrapper invokes `python3 -m egg_agent` (shared/egg_agent/command.py:34), NOT raw `claude -p` — the existing one-shot process model already supports the design, eliminating the need for a custom harness. WS7 keep-warm machinery dropped per the operator's measured ≥60 min cache TTL on both routes. Five open decisions for implement-phase are enumerated (memory layout, memory shape, next-action surface, idle safety budget threshold, daemon fallback).

### [2026-06-01T03:37:20Z] risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from risk_analyst

Risk register for #2908 BRC event-pump + durable memory rewrite. 14 risks identified (3 HIGH/HIGH top-3): R1 spawn-primitive shift (Agent SDK → claude -p one-shot loses MCP registration + system_prompt nudge plumbing), R2 slice-scoped gateway-session keepalive (#2451) + heartbeat (#2036) migration from agent process to wrapper (env path exists via EGG_SLICE_ID, wiring is the risk), R3 server-side next-action correctness across open-NACK barrier (#2142), stale-version (#2482), conditional-ACK (#1998), obligation-resolution (#2338), dual-role ordering (#2749) — all currently prose-prompt rules being migrated to derivation logic. Also surfaces R4 #2741 shell-metachar corruption regression risk if WS8 collapses MCP→CLI without stdin/file prose plumbing (net-new primitive — no --reason-from-stdin exists today), R5 memory-file atomicity (no shared agent-facing helper today; overseer-internal template can be promoted), R7 silent-stall escape if 3-restart cap removed without wall-clock idle budget (existing detectors are state-shape based, don't fire on silent agents). Runtime-primitive audit per #2594 covers 12 primitives (EGG_SLICE_ID EXISTS, --append-system-prompt EXISTS-but-footgun-per-cllm-bug, --reason stdin/file MISSING, atomic-write helper MISSING, next-action MISSING, consensus status --json EXISTS-but-needs-extension). Trust-boundary audit confirms no new boundaries crossed; existing pod/role/gateway substrate untouched per issue scope. Recommendation: PROCEED WITH STAGED ROLLOUT, behind a flag, GATED ON WS0 spike evidence with extended acceptance criteria (slice-id heartbeat verification, MCP/tool registration round-trip, --append-system-prompt order, memory-file atomicity). Six open questions surfaced for plan phase (Open Decisions #1-#4 from issue body plus spawn-primitive pin and WS8 prose-plumbing API shape). No third-party dependency or version bump → no external CVE research applicable; WS7 cache measurements are authoritative.

### [2026-06-01T03:42:11Z] risk_analyst → architect (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from risk_analyst for architect

Reviewed architect-output.json (362 lines) and architect-slices.yaml (96 lines) through the risk lens against my own risk register's top-3 + R4-R14 findings.

### Strong mitigations of my top risks
- **R1 (spawn-primitive shift) — RESOLVED at design level.** Architect EXPLICITLY CORRECTS the issue body's `claude -p` reference: design uses `python3 -m egg_agent` (shared/egg_agent/command.py:34) — the existing Agent SDK wrapper that already supports one-shot. This neutralizes the MCP-registration loss, the `--append-system-prompt` clobber footgun, and the system_prompt_nudge plumbing concerns. Excellent correction, made early.
- **R2 (heartbeat + slice-scoped keepalive migration) — addressed.** Slice-2 goal explicitly migrates `_start_wait_loop_heartbeat` + `slice_id_hb = resolve_slice_id(req)` from handlers/message.py:267-429 into the wrapper. Env path (EGG_SLICE_ID via kubernetes_spawner.py:925) confirmed sufficient.
- **R3 (next-action correctness) — addressed.** od-3 recommends NEW dedicated `brc next-action` endpoint over re-using `consensus status --json` ("pushes sequencing into testable orchestrator code rather than fragile wrapper bash"). Slice-1 verification enumerates "producer/reviewer/dual-role incl. open-NACK barrier (#2142), conditional ACK, stale-version re-review (#2482)" — covers my key cases.
- **R4 (shell-metachar regression #2741) — addressed verbatim.** Slice-5 contains the exact hard constraint I wanted: "prose-bearing CLI commands (consensus propose/ack/nack's reason / files_reviewed args) must accept text via stdin or --file PATH — never argv".
- **R7 (idle/no-progress budget) — addressed.** od-4: 30-min wall-clock budget (well above WS7 10-13 min observed peak).
- **R13 (memory growth) — addressed.** od-2 picks distill-on-write (bounded), citing the WS7 ≥60 min cache TTL as justification that per-event cache-bust cost is bounded.

### Risk-primitive-audit alignment
Architect's `runtime_primitive_assumptions.primitives[]` block independently catalogues 21 primitives with the same in-sandbox-agent / orchestrator pod / litellm sidecar / trusted-CI-runner taxonomy I used. Notable independent finding (corrects my note): architect verified the agent is `python3 -m egg_agent` (not `claude -p`), and additionally that ScriptedProvider (integration_tests/regression/conftest.py + 5 test_brc_*.py files) is the existing E2E ground truth — slice-2/slice-4 must exercise it. Both findings strengthen rather than weaken my register.

### Non-blocking
- **risk_analyst:R5 (memory-file atomicity) — partial.** Architect's mitigation "scope memory to (role, slice)" handles cross-slice collision but does not specify atomic-write semantics for within-pod back-to-back events. Recommend slice-1 acceptance criterion: memory writes use tempfile+os.replace (promote shared/egg_overseer/state.py:265-289 template to a shared helper).
- **risk_analyst:R6 (adversarial re-review weakening) — borderline.** Slice-3 says "evaluate only the delta" but ambiguous whether delta = `changed_artifacts` only OR `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` per REVIEWER-SYNC.md. Recommend slice-3 acceptance criterion: per-event prompt MUST include `last_reviewed_commit_sha` per producer + the full git-log delta, not just the orchestrator's `changed_artifacts` field. Without this the stateless event-pump weakens the "delta as fresh review" mandate.
- **risk_analyst:R8 (cache prefix stability across rollout) — two-window concern.** Slice-3 collapses mission.md + _build_brc_preamble (prefix change #1); slice-5 deletes MCP schemas + SYSTEM_PROMPT_NUDGE (prefix change #2). My R8 mitigation recommended bundling these into one prefix-change window; architect splits them across two slices. Acceptable (WS7 measurements show cache cost is bounded) but worth pinning measurement at each boundary: WS7's cache-cost gate should fire BEFORE slice-3, BETWEEN slice-3 and slice-5, and AFTER slice-5 — three datapoints, not two.
- **risk_analyst:R9 (slice-2 verification missing explicit slice-id assertion).** Slice-2 verification covers "wrapper-side heartbeat emission while blocking on wait-loop" but doesn't explicitly assert the emitted heartbeat carries `slice_id` from `EGG_SLICE_ID`. Recommend extending the slice-2 unit test to: "wrapper-side heartbeat payload has slice_id == os.environ['EGG_SLICE_ID']". One-line assertion, catches the wiring regression directly.
- **Pseudocode inconsistency.** Design pseudocode uses `egg-orch progress complete && exit 0` for role-complete exit; the existing model uses `egg-orch consensus confirmed`. Slice-2 implement should pin: when all peer producers are CONFIRMED and this role's matrix entry says complete, the wrapper calls `consensus confirmed` (which is what generates CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED that other roles wait on). `progress complete` is a different signal type.
- **mission.md scope.** Slice-3 changes mission.md (in the sandbox image) — a sandbox-image rebuild + redeploy cycle. Not blocking but the slice-3 verification should include "agent pod restart picks up new mission.md before flag-flip in slice-4".
- **Slice-5 retired MCP↔CLI drift test.** Architect retires tests/tools/test_mcp_cli_drift.py. Since the entire MCP surface goes away, the drift concept becomes undefined — agreed.

### What I did NOT find blocking
- WS7 cache risks adequately handled (od-5 dropped per measurements).
- Out-of-scope items (orchestrator/mcp_server.py operator-facing MCP, pod/SDK/permissions substrate) correctly excluded.
- Linear 5-slice DAG with no parallel sub-tree fits the forest constraint and slice acceptance work is bounded per slice.

Verdict: ACK. The architecture is sound, the open decisions are pinned with defensible recommendations, the runtime-primitive audit is independent-and-aligned with mine, and the residual non-blockers above are implementation-detail issues that slice-1/2/3 acceptance criteria can address. None rises to a level that would invite an implement-phase NACK if not fixed at plan-phase.

### [2026-06-01T03:43:15Z] reviewer_plan → architect (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for architect


Reviewed `architect-slices.yaml` (5 slices, linear chain) and `architect-output.json` (analysis + design + risks). Independently verified primitive citations: `consensus_wrapper.py:38 MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS`, `:64 _RECOVERY_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, `:116 _CONSENSUS_WRAPPER_TEMPLATE`, `:716 build_consensus_wrapped_command`, `handlers/brc.py:505/586 brc_ack/brc_nack`, `routes/pipelines.py:12348 _build_brc_preamble`, `kubernetes_spawner.py:332/333 JOB_NAME_FORMAT`, `action_guards.py:476 unresolved_nacks`, `routes/signals.py:1169 _stale_version_rejection`, `client.py:300/315/322 MCP registration block`, `command.py build_agent_command` (`python3 -m egg_agent` confirmed; the architect's correction of the issue's `claude -p` reference is correct and important), `egg_overseer/state.py:266/usage_loader.py:95 atomic-write templates`. Trust boundaries (§10) are mostly clean.

### Blocking

1. **Slice-5 sizing violates §11 (hard rubric) — deletion-heavy AND new-API in one cycle.** Slice-5 bundles: (a) ~2,034 LOC of deletion across 11 files (`sandbox/egg_agent_tools/tools/*.py`, server.py NUDGE, client.py:299–353 registration block), (b) net-new stdin/file prose plumbing for `consensus propose --summary`, `consensus ack --reason`/`--files-reviewed`, `consensus nack --reason`/`--files-reviewed` — confirmed missing today (`grep -n "add_argument.*--reason" sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py` shows ALL argv-only at :3265/3485/3573/3600/3647/3660; no `--reason-file` or `--reason -` anywhere), and (c) two net-new CLI subcommands (`brc resolve-obligation`, `brc read-peer-artifact`) with new orchestrator-side routing, plus (d) migration of 3 test files. Per §11: "combines deletion-heavy work with new-API-introduction work — these usually want different review attention and ship better as separate slices." The architect's rationale ("mechanically the same per-file — delete tool + assert handler still reachable via CLI + migrate the matching unit test") is accurate for the deletion side but doesn't cover the additive net-new stdin/file plumbing (which is an API-design concern with a #2741 regression-guard requirement, NOT a mechanical deletion). Risk_analyst R4 reaches the same conclusion from the security axis: "Make stdin/file prose plumbing land BEFORE any MCP tool deprecation in WS8" — i.e. these are different review surfaces that benefit from sequencing.

   **Fix — split slice-5 at the additive/deletion seam (recommended subdivision):**
   - **slice-5a (additive, ships first):** stdin/file plumbing for `propose --summary`/`--summary-file`, `ack --reason`/`--reason-file`/`--files-reviewed-file`, `nack --reason`/`--reason-file`/`--files-reviewed-file`; plus the two net-new CLI subcommands (`brc resolve-obligation`, `brc read-peer-artifact`); plus the #2741 regression guard test asserting prose containing `$VAR`/backticks/`;`/`&&`/embedded newlines round-trips byte-equal via stdin/file. No MCP changes.
   - **slice-5b (deletion-heavy, ships second after 5a clears review):** Delete the 11 tool files, `SYSTEM_PROMPT_NUDGE`, `build_sandbox_mcp_server`, the `client.py:299–353` registration block; retire `tests/tools/test_mcp_cli_drift.py`; migrate `integration_tests/test_sandbox_mcp_tools_e2e.py` (re-purposed per R10 to "first action is `egg-orch consensus ack/nack` via stdin/file, not `bash -c`", NOT just direct-handler) + `tests/sandbox/egg_agent_tools/test_server.py`. Depends on slice-5a so the agent has a non-argv prose path at the moment MCP goes away.

   This ordering is also what risk_analyst R4 mitigation step (1) explicitly recommends and what R10 implies (don't retire the SDK-spawn drift coverage; re-purpose it on top of the stdin/file primitive).

2. **Slice-2 verification claim about ScriptedProvider is structurally wrong (§10 trust-boundary).** `architect-output.json` `verification_strategy.slice_2` states: "Integration: existing BRC concurrency regressions (`integration_tests/regression/test_brc_*.py`) pass with `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP=false` (default) — proving zero regression. With flag on: ScriptedProvider-driven E2E completes consensus without the 3-cap." Both halves of this are incorrect: (a) `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py:1-25` is explicit — "`ScriptedProvider` can't drive deployed agent pods (see #2474), so the tests exercise the BRC Python API directly" via `PeerConsensusTracker` — these tests don't touch the wrapper AT ALL, so they neither prove regression nor zero-regression of `consensus_wrapper.py`; (b) per #2474, ScriptedProvider is unit-test-only (in-process), it cannot drive a deployed event-pump in an agent pod end-to-end. The risk_analyst's primitive audit reaches the same conclusion ("ScriptedProvider test double... trusted-CI-runner ONLY. NOT loaded in deployed pods").

   **Fix:** Re-state slice-2 integration verification as: (i) a new wrapper-rendering unit test that snapshots the bash emitted for `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP=true` and asserts the expected wait-filter set + heartbeat invocation site; (ii) the `PeerConsensusTracker` in-process regressions (the actual `test_brc_*.py` suite) continue to pass — establishing only that orchestrator-side BRC logic is untouched, NOT that the wrapper is verified; (iii) deferring true end-to-end validation of the event pump to slice-4 (the spike on issue-2270/qwen3.7-max). Slice-2 cannot use ScriptedProvider as a verification primitive — only slice-4's real-LLM repro can validate the wrapper end-to-end. (The `integration_tests/regression/README.md` even calls this out at line 85: "Slice DAG reaches `PR_READY` end-to-end with a mid-flight `restart_agent`" is deferred to #2585 precisely because "Requires real BRC consensus driving against a Claude provider, which a fresh CI runner cannot drive.")

### Non-blocking

- **`design.execution_loop_pseudocode` uses a non-existent flag (`--append-context brc-memory.md`).** `shared/egg_agent/command.py:11-46 build_agent_command` exposes `--model`, `--max-turns`, `--system-prompt`, and the positional prompt arg only — no `--append-context`. The architect correctly identifies (line 99 of output) that the existing entry point is `python3 -m egg_agent`, not `claude -p`, but then writes pseudo-code that uses a flag the existing entry point doesn't have. This is illustrative-only at the architecture level, but if task_planner takes it as a pinned interface, slice-3 will spawn a TASK-X-Y to add `--append-context` that nobody architected for. **Suggestion:** state the design-level mechanism explicitly ("memory delivered via composed `--system-prompt` content from the existing flag — preserves cache stability if the memory occupies a stable prefix position; or inlined into the user prompt at a deterministic tail position; OR a net-new `--append-context` flag to be designed in slice-1 if neither suffices") so slice-3 task decomposition isn't surprised by a missing primitive.

- **Slice-1 doesn't surface atomicity for memory writes (risk_analyst R5).** `shared/egg_overseer/state.py:266 _persist_atomic_template` and `shared/egg_contracts/usage_loader.py:95 _atomic_write` both exist as proven templates but neither is sandbox-facing. Slice-1 goal text says "Wire action-scaffolded memory writes into the existing `brc_ack`/`brc_nack` handlers" without committing to atomic write semantics. Per R5 the wrapper-driven event pump invokes the per-event handler more often than the persistent-wait model (every CONSENSUS_PROPOSE/RE_REVIEW/ACK/NACK/STATUS wakeup) so the per-role within-pod serialization window matters. **Suggestion:** add to slice-1 goal: "memory writes use atomic tempfile + os.replace (re-using the `shared/egg_overseer/state.py:266` template or a shared promotion of it); spec verifies no partial-write under back-to-back invocation."

- **Slice-1 doesn't preserve adversarial re-review (risk_analyst R6).** Slice-1's memory schema description ("structured (codebase/change model, per-producer assessment, decision log)") is too coarse. Per R6, the schema MUST include `last_reviewed_commit_sha` per producer so the per-event handler can be handed `git log {last_reviewed_commit_sha}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md) and audit the delta as a fresh review with no NACK history — not just "memory says no concerns + small delta → ACK." If the schema doesn't include this and slice-3 only hands `changed_artifacts`, re-reviewers will systematically miss issues outside their prior NACK scope. **Suggestion:** add `last_reviewed_commit_sha` (per producer) as a required memory-schema field in slice-1 goal, and add to slice-3 goal that the per-event prompt MUST include the `git log {last_reviewed_commit_sha}..HEAD` delta, not just `changed_artifacts`.

- **Spike-after-code-write timing is defensible but inverts the issue's stated rollout.** Issue body: "**0. De-risking spike (gates everything).**" + "Spike (0) → next-action (3) → memory (2) → wrapper + liveness (1, 4) → …". Architect's interpretation builds slices 1-3 flag-gated then validates via slice-4 spike. This is reversible (flag-gated, old path retained) but means slices 1-3 worth of engineering may be wasted if the spike falsifies the design. The architect notes this risk in `risks_and_mitigations[0]`. **Suggestion (non-blocking):** consider an explicit `slice-0` (or document slice-4 as the spike-gate with a "ROLLBACK PLAN" stating: spike-falsified-revert = drop the flag-gated paths via `git revert` of slices 1-3 commits). If you keep the current shape, OK — just be explicit that "spike validates the production code path" is the architectural call.

- **`shared/egg_agent/client.py:316 SYSTEM_PROMPT_NUDGE import` and `:319 build_sandbox_mcp_server()` call** are gated by `EGG_MCP_TOOLS` (default-on since #1942 per line 300 comment). Slice-5b's deletion of these primitives must also remove the env-flag check entirely or risk leaving dead code paths. Architect lists deletion of "shared/egg_agent/client.py:299–353" which covers the whole guarded block — explicit but easy to miss the env-flag check at :311. Confirm in task decomposition.

- **Open-decision od-1 (memory layout subdirectory vs flat).** Architect recommends per-role subdirectory. Risk_analyst R14 raises path-construction footgun if `EGG_AGENT_ROLE` is empty. Both are right. **Suggestion:** if you keep the subdirectory layout, slice-1 task acceptance criterion must include "path construction fails closed if `EGG_AGENT_ROLE` is unset or empty."

Re-propose with slice-5 split into 5a (additive: stdin/file plumbing + 2 new CLI commands) and 5b (deletion-heavy: MCP tool removal + test migration), and slice-2's verification re-stated to use wrapper-rendering unit tests + in-process `PeerConsensusTracker` regressions + slice-4-spike-as-E2E-gate (NOT ScriptedProvider for end-to-end). The non-blocking notes above can be folded in if convenient; they're suggestions rather than gates.

      \ nor zero-regression of `consensus_wrapper.py`; (b) per #2474, ScriptedProvider\
      \ is unit-test-only (in-process), it cannot drive a deployed event-pump in an\
      \ agent pod end-to-end. The risk_analyst's primitive audit reaches the same\
      \ conclusion (\"ScriptedProvider test double... trusted-CI-runner ONLY. NOT\
      \ loaded in deployed pods\").\n\n   **Fix:** Re-state slice-2 integration verification\
      \ as: (i) a new wrapper-rendering unit test that snapshots the bash emitted\
      \ for `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP=true` and asserts the expected wait-filter set + heartbeat\

      \ actual `test_brc_*.py` suite) continue to pass \u2014 establishing only that\
      \ orchestrator-side BRC logic is untouched, NOT that the wrapper is verified;\
      \ (iii) deferring true end-to-end validation of the event pump to slice-4 (the\
      \ spike on issue-2270/qwen3.7-max). Slice-2 cannot use ScriptedProvider as a\
      \ verification primitive \u2014 only slice-4's real-LLM repro can validate the\
      \ wrapper end-to-end. (The `integration_tests/regression/README.md` even calls\
      \ this out at line 85: \"Slice DAG reaches `PR_READY` end-to-end with a mid-flight\
      \ `restart_agent`\" is deferred to #2585 precisely because \"Requires real BRC\
      \ consensus driving against a Claude provider, which a fresh CI runner cannot\
      \ drive.\")\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n- **`design.execution_loop_pseudocode` uses\
      \ a non-existent flag (`--append-context brc-memory.md`).** `shared/egg_agent/command.py:11-46\
      \ build_agent_command` exposes `--model`, `--max-turns`, `--system-prompt`,\
      \ and the positional prompt arg only \u2014 no `--append-context`. The architect\
      \ correctly identifies (line 99 of output) that the existing entry point is\
      \ `python3 -m egg_agent`, not `claude -p`, but then writes pseudo-code that\
      \ uses a flag the existing entry point doesn't have. This is illustrative-only\
      \ at the architecture level, but if task_planner takes it as a pinned interface,\
      \ slice-3 will spawn a TASK-X-Y to add `--append-context` that nobody architected\
      \ for. **Suggestion:** state the design-level mechanism explicitly (\"memory\
      \ delivered via composed `--system-prompt` content from the existing flag \u2014\
      \ preserves cache stability if the memory occupies a stable prefix position;\
      \ or inlined into the user prompt at a deterministic tail position; OR a net-new\
      \ `--append-context` flag to be designed in slice-1 if neither suffices\") so\
      \ slice-3 task decomposition isn't surprised by a missing primitive.\n\n- **Slice-1\
      \ doesn't surface atomicity for memory writes (risk_analyst R5).** `shared/egg_overseer/state.py:266\
      \ _persist_atomic_template` and `shared/egg_contracts/usage_loader.py:95 _atomic_write`\
      \ both exist as proven templates but neither is sandbox-facing. Slice-1 goal\
      \ text says \"Wire action-scaffolded memory writes into the existing `brc_ack`/`brc_nack`\
      \ handlers\" without committing to atomic write semantics. Per R5 the wrapper-driven\
      \ event pump invokes the per-event handler more often than the persistent-wait\
      \ model (every CONSENSUS_PROPOSE/RE_REVIEW/ACK/NACK/STATUS wakeup) so the per-role\
      \ within-pod serialization window matters. **Suggestion:** add to slice-1 goal:\
      \ \"memory writes use atomic tempfile + os.replace (re-using the `shared/egg_overseer/state.py:266`\
      \ template or a shared promotion of it); spec verifies no partial-write under\
      \ back-to-back invocation.\"\n\n- **Slice-1 doesn't preserve adversarial re-review\
      \ (risk_analyst R6).** Slice-1's memory schema description (\"structured (codebase/change\
      \ model, per-producer assessment, decision log)\") is too coarse. Per R6, the\
      \ schema MUST include `last_reviewed_commit_sha` per producer so the per-event\
      \ handler can be handed `git log {last_reviewed_commit_sha}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch}\
      \ -p` (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md) and audit the delta as a fresh review with no NACK\
      \ history \u2014 not just \"memory says no concerns + small delta \u2192 ACK.\"\
      \ If the schema doesn't include this and slice-3 only hands `changed_artifacts`,\
      \ re-reviewers will systematically miss issues outside their prior NACK scope.\
      \ **Suggestion:** add `last_reviewed_commit_sha` (per producer) as a required\
      \ memory-schema field in slice-1 goal, and add to slice-3 goal that the per-event\
      \ prompt MUST include the `git log {last_reviewed_commit_sha}..HEAD` delta,\
      \ not just `changed_artifacts`.\n\n- **Spike-after-code-write timing is defensible\
      \ but inverts the issue's stated rollout.** Issue body: \"**0. De-risking spike\

      \ risk in `risks_and_mitigations[0]`. **Suggestion (non-blocking):** consider\

      \ Both are right. **Suggestion:** if you keep the subdirectory layout, slice-1\
      \ task acceptance criterion must include \"path construction fails closed if\
      \ `EGG_AGENT_ROLE` is unset or empty.\"\n\nRe-propose with slice-5 split into\

### [2026-06-01T03:49:56Z] architect (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from architect

v2 architecture analysis + 6-slice scaffold. Addresses BOTH blockers in reviewer_plan v1 NACK and folds in BOTH reviewers' non-blockers. (Blocker 1, reviewer_plan + risk_analyst R4) Former slice-5 split at additive/deletion seam: slice-5 = additive stdin/file prose plumbing for `consensus propose/ack/nack` + 2 new BRC CLI subcommands (`brc resolve-obligation`, `brc read-peer-artifact`) + #2741 regression-guard test; slice-6 = mechanical deletion of 28 MCP tools + SYSTEM_PROMPT_NUDGE + EGG_MCP_TOOLS flag at client.py:311 + test migration (e2e exercise preserved as stdin/file `consensus ack/nack`). (Blocker 2, reviewer_plan) slice-2 verification rewritten — ScriptedProvider is unit-test-only and cannot drive a deployed event pump end-to-end (per integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py:1-25 and #2474); slice-2 now verifies via wrapper-rendering snapshot tests + slice_id-propagation unit-test assertion (risk_analyst R9) + in-process PeerConsensusTracker regressions, with true end-to-end deferred to slice-4 spike. Non-blockers folded: new §design.memory_schema with `last_reviewed_commit_sha` per producer (risk_analyst R6 + reviewer_plan adversarial-re-review), atomic-write contract via tempfile+os.replace (shared/egg_overseer/state.py:266 helper), fail-closed path construction; slice-3 acceptance pins full `git log {last_reviewed_commit_sha}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` delta in per-event prompt; pseudocode corrected (`consensus confirmed` not `progress complete`; `--append-context` flagged illustrative-only + new od-6 to pin memory-delivery mechanism); slice-3 verification adds sandbox-image rebuild + agent restart for mission.md before slice-4 flag-flip; three-point WS7 cache measurement schedule (baseline / post-slice-3 / post-slice-6); explicit rollback plan in slice-4. Changed artifacts: both architect-output.json and architect-slices.yaml.

**Adversarial re-review**

**Your v2 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v1 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the delta since your last review (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md: `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` — the commits landed since the version you last verdicted, NOT the whole accumulated proposal surface) as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v1 NACK history. Read that diff as if you'd never seen the prior version. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied. **Mandate 2 is bounded to this delta** — it does NOT ask you to re-traverse the whole accumulated surface from earlier cycles; that work was amortized when you first reviewed those commits.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has newly-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has newly-introduced races, contract has newly-introduced AC drift, code has newly-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did prior issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does this delta introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, this delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v2 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from that diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v2 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v1 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.

### [2026-06-01T03:53:06Z] risk_analyst → architect (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from risk_analyst for architect

Re-ACK at v3 — orchestrator rejected my v2 ACK as stale because architect re-proposed to v3 at the same commit (6342b2d7a). Code delta against v1 is unchanged from what I reviewed for v2; my v2 review applies verbatim.

Reviewed v3 as adversarial re-review per the dual-mandate framing — both (1) my v1 non-blockers are addressed AND (2) audited the delta b6088e988..6342b2d7a as a fresh review (113 insertions / 54 deletions across the two architect artifacts).

### v1 non-blockers — all addressed in v3
- **R5 (memory atomicity) — ADDRESSED.** New `§design.memory_schema.atomic_write_contract`: tempfile + os.replace via promoted helper from shared/egg_overseer/state.py:266 or shared/egg_contracts/usage_loader.py:95. Slice-1 acceptance criterion + dedicated risks_and_mitigations line.
- **R6 (delta-scope) — ADDRESSED.** Slice-3 verification pins full `git log {last_reviewed_commit_sha}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` delta per producer, NOT just `changed_artifacts`. memory_schema includes `last_reviewed_commit_sha` (mechanically derivable, drift-resistant). Dedicated risks_and_mitigations line.
- **R8 (cache-prefix stability) — ADDRESSED.** Three-point WS7 measurement schedule (baseline / post-slice-3 / post-slice-6); regression > 20% triggers HITL pause.
- **R9 (slice-2 slice_id assertion) — ADDRESSED EXACTLY.** Slice-2 verification (ii): wrapper-side heartbeat unit test asserts `slice_id == os.environ['EGG_SLICE_ID']`.
- **R10 (test migration) — ADDRESSED.** Slice-6 re-purposes test_sandbox_mcp_tools_e2e.py to exercise stdin/file consensus ack/nack — preserves SDK-spawn coverage.
- **R14 (fail-closed path) — ADDRESSED.** memory_schema.fail_closed_path_construction raises if EGG_AGENT_ROLE empty.
- **Pseudocode `consensus confirmed` correction — ADDRESSED.** Inline comment clarifies the agent calls consensus confirmed on final invocation.
- **mission.md sandbox-image rebuild — ADDRESSED.** Slice-3 verification pins rebuild + restart before flag-flip in slice-4.

### Reviewer_plan v1 blockers also addressed
- Slice-5/6 split at additive/deletion seam (matches my R4 ordering recommendation independently).
- Slice-2 verification corrected: ScriptedProvider is unit-test-only per integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py:1-25 + #2474; slice-2 uses wrapper-rendering snapshot tests + slice_id assertion + in-process PeerConsensusTracker, with true E2E deferred to slice-4 spike. Legitimate correction I missed in v1.

### Delta audit (fresh review beyond v1 mitigations)
- **od-6 added** honestly flagging `--append-context` does NOT exist in build_agent_command (shared/egg_agent/command.py:11-46) — only --model/--max-turns/--system-prompt/positional. Three options outlined (A: --system-prompt, B: tail-inline, C: net-new --memory-file); recommends B. Right kind of plan-phase honesty — don't pin a non-existent primitive.
- **Six-slice DAG linear**, no parallel sub-tree.
- **Default EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP=false until slice-4** — no production traffic on new path; revert slices 1-3 via git revert if spike falsifies.
- **memory_schema Decision log capped at last 20 entries** — reasonable; configurable in implement-phase if needed.
- **No new high-severity risks introduced.** v3 strictly improves on v1: no scope creep, no inverted ordering, no new primitive assumptions.

### Non-blocking (light suggestions, no fix expected)
- Slice-2 heartbeat assertion should also cover the no-slice case (EGG_SLICE_ID unset for plan/refine phases): assert wrapper either emits without slice_id OR with explicit null/empty — not a literal `"$EGG_SLICE_ID"` from un-substituted bash.
- memory_schema Decision log cap at 20 could be made configurable per phase if churning re-review cycle hits the limit.

Verdict: ACK v3. Architecture is plan-ready. All risk-lens concerns from v1 addressed verbatim; reviewer_plan blockers addressed; delta audit surfaces no new blockers and no implement-phase NACK trajectories. Verification strategy is both more specific (slice_id assertion, git-log delta, three-point cache measurement, atomic-write helper) AND more honest (slice-2 ScriptedProvider correction, od-6 --append-context illustrative-only).

### [2026-06-01T03:56:34Z] reviewer_plan → architect (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for architect


Re-ACKing at v3. Architect's v3 metadata advances the proposal version but the commit_sha (6342b2d7a) is identical to v2 — no content delta between v2 and v3 (verified `git diff` is empty on the proposal artifacts at the new HEAD vs the v2 HEAD; both proposals reference the same commit). My v2 review applies verbatim. Full review (mandates 1 + 2 explicitly enumerated, with verified primitive checks for `EGG_MCP_TOOLS` at client.py:311, `_persist_atomic_template` at egg_overseer/state.py:266, `_atomic_write` at usage_loader.py:95, the `ScriptedProvider` trust-boundary at test_brc_concurrency.py:19-20, `last_reviewed_commit` existing identifier at routes/pipelines.py:6296/peer_consensus.py:1531, and the mission.md dual-path situation at sandbox/agent-config/rules/ vs sandbox/claude-rules/Dockerfile:213) inlined below.

### Mandate 1 — v1 blockers verified-fixed (still hold at v3)

(a) **Slice-5 sizing — additive/deletion seam split (FIXED).** Slice-5 v3 is purely additive (stdin/`--reason-file`/`--summary-file`/`--files-reviewed-file` plumbing on existing argv sites at orch_cli.py:3265/3485/3573/3600/3647/3660 + two net-new BRC subcommands `brc resolve-obligation` / `brc read-peer-artifact` + #2741 regression-guard test). Slice-6 v3 is mechanical deletion (11 tool files + `SYSTEM_PROMPT_NUDGE` + `build_sandbox_mcp_server` + client.py:299–353 MCP registration block including `EGG_MCP_TOOLS` env-flag check at :311 — no orphan flag). slice-6 depends on slice-5 so the agent has a non-argv prose path at MCP deletion time. Matches risk_analyst R4 sequencing exactly and resolves the §11 deletion-heavy+new-API combination.

(b) **Slice-2 verification ScriptedProvider claim (FIXED).** v3's `verification_strategy.slice_2` explicitly states ScriptedProvider is unit-test-only and cannot drive a deployed pod (citing `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py:1-25` and #2474). Substitute verification: (i) wrapper-rendering snapshot of the bash for `EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP=true` asserting wait-filter + heartbeat invocation + idle-budget threshold, (ii) wrapper-side heartbeat unit test asserting `slice_id == os.environ['EGG_SLICE_ID']` (folds in risk_analyst R9), (iii) in-process `PeerConsensusTracker` regression suite as orchestrator-side invariance only, (iv) true E2E deferred to slice-4 spike. Trust boundary (§10) correctly partitioned.

**Non-blockers folded:** new `§design.memory_schema` (last_reviewed_commit_sha per producer + atomic-write contract + fail-closed path construction); pseudocode now extensively commented as illustrative with three memory-delivery options (A/B/C); new `od-6` to pin the memory-delivery mechanism; `progress complete` → `exit 0` with `consensus confirmed` comment; slice-3 verification adds sandbox-image rebuild + agent restart for mission.md before slice-4 flag-flip; three-point WS7 cache measurement schedule (baseline / post-slice-3 / post-slice-6); explicit slice-4 rollback plan ("revert slices 1-3 via `git revert`"); slice-1 `EGG_AGENT_ROLE` fail-closed pinned; risks_and_mitigations adds "Adversarial re-review weakening" + "Cache-prefix invalidation across rollout" with concrete mitigations.

### Mandate 2 — Fresh-reviewer audit of v2→v3 delta

The v2→v3 delta is empty (same commit_sha; the version advance appears to be a metadata-only re-propose by the architect, likely after my stale 409 surfaced or as part of orchestrator-side housekeeping). Per the "bounded to this delta" framing, an empty delta has nothing for a fresh reviewer to find. I executed the dual-mandate framing explicitly: imagined a reviewer with no v2 NACK history reading ONLY the diff between v2 and v3 commits (empty) — there is no new hunk to apply the rubric to. Mandate 2 vacuously passes; mandate 1 still applies as the substantive ACK basis.

For completeness, the v1→v2 non-blocking findings I raised at my (rejected) v2 ACK still stand as advisory notes for task_planner's plan-decomposition phase — they are NOT regressions or new issues, and they are NOT blocking. Re-listed verbatim so the audit trail captures them on the accepted verdict:

### Non-blocking (carried over from v1→v2 audit; not blocking)

- **§9 mission.md citation precision — `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` is NOT what the sandbox image actually reads.** `sandbox/Dockerfile:213` reads `COPY sandbox/claude-rules/*.md /opt/claude-rules/` — only `sandbox/claude-rules/` flows into the deployed pod's `/opt/claude-rules/`. Both `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` and `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` exist as byte-identical regular files today (12,195 bytes each, same mtime, both tracked under the same git history) and are maintained as duplicates in lockstep. If slice-3 implement-phase edits ONLY the architect-cited `agent-config/rules/` path, the sandbox image rebuild copies the OLD content. The slice-3 verification step ("sandbox-image rebuild picks up the new mission.md") would catch the failure reactively. **Suggestion (task_planner):** make slice-3 modify-list cite BOTH paths and add an acceptance criterion `diff sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` returns empty after the change. (`sandbox/AGENTS.md:7` docs `agent-config/rules/` as canonical while Dockerfile reads `claude-rules/` — pre-existing organizational tension; this issue doesn't have to resolve it but should not be caught off-guard by it.)

- **§9 `last_reviewed_commit_sha` naming inconsistency with existing orchestrator identifier `last_reviewed_commit`.** Existing code uses `last_reviewed_commit` at routes/pipelines.py:6296/6341/6345/12854/13204/13210/13256/13262 and peer_consensus.py:1531. Architect's memory-schema uses `last_reviewed_commit_sha`. **Suggestion (task_planner):** rename memory-schema field to `last_reviewed_commit` to match the existing orchestrator identifier; or document the rename rationale in §design.memory_schema.

- **Slice-5 argv "fallback during transition" not removed in slice-6.** Slice-5 goal says "keep argv accepted as a fallback during transition." Slice-6 doesn't close the argv prose acceptance path. So argv prose remains permanently accepted past slice-6 — technically consistent with the design but soft-violates the issue's "never argv" hard constraint at #2741. **Suggestion (task_planner / architect):** either (a) document argv stays accepted indefinitely as a back-compat tradeoff with the in-pod agent prompted to prefer stdin/file, OR (b) add a slice-6 task to deprecate argv with a warning, OR (c) attach a follow-up issue tag. Any of the three; current shape leaves the question open.

- **od-6 Option B + memory_schema `distill-on-write` interact unfavorably with WS7's stable-prefix measurement envelope.** WS7 measured CROSS-SESSION caching of a STABLE prefix (60-min TTL on both routes, 92-99.99% cache_read fraction). `distill-on-write` means the memory file is rewritten each event; Option B (memory inline at user-prompt tail) means the prompt's tail changes each event. Cache providers typically key on prefix-byte-stability — the per-event memory-tail-inline scenario is outside the WS7 measurement envelope. The architect's 20% regression budget at the three-point measurement schedule may be tight. **Suggestion (slice-3 implement-phase):** add a fourth measurement point (synthetic two-event sequence with same prefix, distinct memory tails) to validate Option B's design assumption empirically before slice-3 commits to it. If Option B regresses > 20%, fall back to Option C (net-new `--memory-file` flag with stable per-prompt position) so cache is broken once per memory-write, not once per event.

- **Slice-3 prompt collapse should explicitly preserve the dual-mandate adversarial re-review banner.** `routes/pipelines.py:13204-13210` carries the "(1) verify prior blockers + (2) audit delta as fresh reviewer" banner that risk_analyst R6 anchors the adversarial re-review around. Architect's slice-3 collapses the STAY-ALIVE / wait-loop mechanics / cursor-threading text, which is correct; but if the dual-mandate banner is collapsed-out alongside the STAY-ALIVE block, the BEHAVIORAL framing that tells the agent "two mandates, both weighted equally" disappears. The `§design.memory_schema` last_reviewed_commit + slice-3 verification "FULL git-log delta in per-event prompt" cover the data side, NOT the behavioral framing. **Suggestion (slice-3 implement-phase):** add to slice-3 acceptance criterion: "the dual-mandate re-review framing at routes/pipelines.py:13204-13210 is RETAINED in the collapsed preamble (not part of the STAY-ALIVE / wait-loop mechanics removal scope)."

### Summary

Mandate 1 still passes at v3 (no content change; v1 blockers remain fixed). Mandate 2 vacuously passes on the empty v2→v3 delta. Non-blockers are advisory for task_planner's plan decomposition. ACKing.

### [2026-06-01T03:58:56Z] reviewer_plan → risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for risk_analyst


Reviewed `risk_analyst-output.json` (493 lines, 14 risks R1-R14, runtime_primitive_audit_per_2594 with 11 primitives, trust_boundary_audit with 5 boundaries, rollback_plan per workstream, complexity_assessment, areas_requiring_human_review, open_questions_for_plan_phase Q1-Q6, external_research_sources). Verified citations: `handlers/message.py:234 _start_wait_loop_heartbeat`, `:321 resolve_slice_id(req)`, `orchestrator/health_checks/tier1/{consensus_stall.py,incomplete_consensus_stall.py}` (both exist), `orchestrator/overseer/monitor.py:213 _classify_stall` (also at :428, :541), `sandbox/egg_lib/sdlc_hitl.py:430` (single `--append-system-prompt` use site), `sandbox/llm/runner.py:18` (docstring reference). Cross-checked against the architect's v3 proposal — risk_analyst's R4/R5/R6/R9/R10/R14 are all directly reflected in architect's slice scaffold + design.memory_schema + verification_strategy. Productive collaboration.

### Structural quality

- **Severity calibration is reasonable.** Top-3 risks all HIGH/HIGH (spawn-primitive shift, liveness migration, server-side next-action) with concrete impact descriptions tied to specific failure modes (gateway session reaping, silent mis-sequencing, MCP/system-prompt drop). MEDIUMs are appropriately scoped to operational/rollout concerns. LOWs cover edge cases (cross-role path collision, memory-file growth without compaction).
- **Mitigation specificity is high.** Each R has an effort estimate (LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH), residual-risk rating, and concrete test specifications where applicable — e.g. R2 ("wrapper-side heartbeat unit test asserts every emitted heartbeat carries `slice_id` from `EGG_SLICE_ID` env"), R4 ("regression guard test that round-trips a string containing `$VAR`, backticks, `;`, `&&`, embedded newlines, and quote characters via the new primitive and asserts byte-equality after gateway parse").
- **runtime_primitive_audit_per_2594** correctly categorizes primitives as EXISTS / EXISTS but FOOTGUN / MISSING with concrete file:line evidence. The footgun flag on `--append-system-prompt` (Claude Code honors only the LAST one — pre-empts a real implementation pitfall) is exactly the level of detail #2594 was added for.
- **trust_boundary_audit** correctly partitions agent_pod→orchestrator, wrapper→orchestrator, memory_file_within_pod, shared_event_message_types, mcp_tool_schemas_in_cache_prefix with risk levels and rationale. The "lockstep_required: YES" call-out on shared event message types ("WS3 next-action MUST handle every shape the wait-loop currently filters on, OR explicitly document which are wrapper-only") is a strong defensive observation.
- **operator_directives_acknowledged** correctly captures all six WS7 measurements + the #2741 hard constraint + the orchestrator-MCP out-of-scope clarification.
- **External research** correctly cites the three operator comments on #2908 (Anthropic/Qwen cache measurements, TTL ceiling sweep, cllm `--append-system-prompt` bug) with concrete summaries.

### Non-blocking observations

- **R1 `requires_human_review` references "Open Decision #2" but #2 is about `--resume`, not spawn primitive.** The issue body's open decision 2 is "SDK `--resume` for the tightly-coupled NACK→re-review thread only (cheap raw continuity) vs uniform fresh+memory everywhere." R1 is about the spawn-primitive shift (Agent SDK options vs `claude -p` CLI flags) which is a SEPARATE concern. risk_analyst's `areas_requiring_human_review[4]` correctly names "Spawn primitive choice (`python3 -m egg_agent` Agent SDK vs `claude -p` per-event one-shot)" as a distinct item, so the substantive concern is captured. But R1's `review_reason` cross-reference to OD-2 is mis-mapped — the issue has no explicit open decision for spawn primitive. **Suggestion (risk_analyst, future cycle):** clarify R1's review_reason to point at the new (architect-introduced) `od-6` (memory-delivery mechanism) or call this out as a missing operator decision rather than mis-anchoring on OD-2. (Non-blocking because the architect's v3 explicitly commits to `python3 -m egg_agent` — the substantive concern is already resolved in the proposal.)

- **R6 mitigation introduces `last_reviewed_commit_sha` naming, but existing orchestrator code uses `last_reviewed_commit` (no `_sha` suffix).** Verified at `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:6296` (`last_reviewed_commit: str | None = None`), `:6341/6345/12854/13204/13210/13256/13262` and `orchestrator/peer_consensus.py:1531` ("reviewer-self-tracked `last_reviewed_commit` range"). risk_analyst's R6 mitigation step (1) introduces `last_reviewed_commit_sha`, which the architect then adopted in `§design.memory_schema`. The `_sha` suffix is more descriptive but introduces a parallel identifier. **Suggestion (risk_analyst, future cycle):** when recommending a schema field name in a mitigation, check the existing orchestrator identifier first — this prevents downstream API drift. Same suggestion applied to architect in my ACK there.

- **R3 mitigation step (3) recommends "shadow mode" parity testing that architect didn't commit to.** R3's suggested approach: "Gate WS3 on a 'derivation matches preamble semantics' parity test (start with both old + new running in shadow mode, alert on divergence)." Shadow mode is the gold standard for high-blast-radius derivation logic, but it adds substantial implementation complexity (two code paths running in parallel + divergence telemetry). Architect's slice-1 verification covers the derivation cases unit-test-by-unit-test (producer/reviewer/dual-role with each #2142/#2482/#1998/#2338/#2749/#2064 shape) which is a reasonable substitute for high-coverage unit tests, but doesn't catch production-shape divergence. **Suggestion (task_planner phase):** if the per-cycle latency / cost budget permits, slice-1 could add a shadow-mode parity check as an opt-in dev-mode env flag — risk_analyst R3 names this as the recommended safety net for the derivation surface. Non-blocking because the unit-test coverage architect committed to is the minimum bar.

- **R8 mitigation step (2) recommends "Sequence WS6 (prompt collapse) and WS8 (MCP→CLI collapse) into a SINGLE prefix-change window" — but architect chose two windows.** Architect's slice-3 (prompt collapse) and slice-6 (MCP schema removal) are two separate prefix-change events. R8's recommendation was "one cold read on rollout, not two." Architect's three-point measurement schedule (baseline / post-slice-3 / post-slice-6) accepts the two-window reality and gates each boundary on `cache_read` regression < 20%. This is a defensible alternative (two measured rollouts vs one larger rollout) — risk_analyst's recommendation is the stronger ask but the architect's measurement gate is sound. Non-blocking; calling out so task_planner knows architect chose differently than risk_analyst recommended.

- **R11 + R12 mitigations recommend specific unit tests architect's slice verification doesn't explicitly enumerate.** R11 ("dual-role agent in WORKING state with peer producer CONSENSUS_PROPOSE pending → next-action MUST be 'propose', not 'review'") and R12 ("Wrapper-side wait construction: pre-confirm waits OMIT CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED; post-confirm waits INCLUDE it") are both concrete derivation-correctness shapes that fall under architect's slice-1 next-action unit-test surface but aren't individually named in `verification_strategy.slice_1`. Architect's wording ("brc next-action derivation across producer/reviewer/dual-role incl. open-NACK barrier (#2142), conditional ACK, stale-version re-review (#2482)") covers the categories but doesn't enumerate R11/R12's specific shapes. **Suggestion (task_planner phase):** task_planner's slice-1 task acceptance criteria should explicitly enumerate R11 + R12 cases (dual-role pre/post-propose + pre/post-confirm wait construction). Architect's wording leaves it implicit; risk_analyst's explicit list is the bar.

- **R12 references `--for STATUS` "wakes the producer (#2531)".** I didn't independently verify #2531 against the codebase — the reference is contextual (the wait-loop filter set behavior is well-established). Risk_analyst's substantive concern (event-pump must classify pre-confirm CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED as a filter to OMIT, not INCLUDE) is sound regardless. Non-blocking.

### Summary

Risk register is high-quality, calibration is reasonable, primitive citations verified, mitigations are specific and testable. The architect's v3 proposal explicitly folds in R4/R5/R6/R9/R10/R14 (and partially R8 via three-point measurement). R11/R12 mitigations should be enumerated in task_planner's slice-1 task ACs. R1/R6 naming consistency notes are advisory for future risk_analyst cycles. None of the observations blocks consensus.

### [2026-06-01T04:04:07Z] risk_analyst → task_planner (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from risk_analyst for task_planner

Reviewed plan.md (1620 lines) through the risk lens. Spot-verified the four risk-lens NACK candidates directly in the file before deciding: each turns out to be a non-blocking nit, not a blocker.

### Strong coverage of risk register mitigations

Verified each of my risk register's testable mitigations IS pinned in concrete task ACs:
- **R5 atomic-write** — TASK-1-6 AC line 616-619 + TASK-1-9 line 676-680 (atomic-write contract under fault injection).
- **R14 fail-closed path** — TASK-1-6 line 618-620 + TASK-1-9 line 679-680 (constructor raises on empty EGG_AGENT_ROLE).
- **R9 slice_id heartbeat assertion** — TASK-2-2 line 781-785 + TASK-2-6 (iii) line 875-877 (one test pins this directly).
- **R4 #2741 shell-metachar** — TASK-5-5 lines 1378-1400 ($VAR / backticks / $() / ; / && / newlines / UTF-8 round-trip).
- **R6 git-log delta** — TASK-3-1 line 948 (full `git log {sha}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p`) + TASK-3-6 line 1086-1088.
- **R7 idle budget** — TASK-2-3 (replaces MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS with EGG_BRC_IDLE_BUDGET_MIN, default 30 min per architect od-4).
- **R8 three-point WS7 measurement** — TASK-3-7 (#1) + TASK-4-1 (#2) + TASK-6-7 (#3) with 20% regression threshold.
- **R10 SDK-spawn preserved** — TASK-6-4 line 1530-1532 (e2e re-purposed to exercise stdin/file consensus ack/nack, NOT collapsed to direct-handler).
- **mission.md sandbox-image rebuild** — TASK-3-4 line 1045-1051 (rebuild + tag recorded in PR body so slice-4 can verify before flag-flip).
- **Memory schema with last_reviewed_commit_sha + decision-log cap at 20** — TASK-1-6 ACs lines 594-610.
- **EGG_BRC_MEMORY off/write-only/full mode matrix** — TASK-1-6 + TASK-3-2 (with default progression off→write-only at slice-1, write-only→full at slice-4).
- **EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP gate + byte-for-byte legacy template** — TASK-2-1 acceptance line 758-761.
- **409 stale_version handled as re-fetch not retry** — TASK-2-1 lines 751-753 + TASK-2-6 (vi) line 879-880.
- **Slice ordering 5 before 6** (additive prose plumbing before deletion) — explicit dependency: slice-6 depends on slice-5.

### Non-blocking — light AC tightening

1. **R11 dual-role ordering under-enumerated.** TASK-1-2 line 529 and TASK-1-10 line 695 list "dual-role mid-review" as one of seven derivation cases. R11 actually requires two distinct sub-cases — (a) dual-role with own un-proposed producer edge + pending review on different edge → next-action MUST order `propose` before `ack/nack` per #2749; (b) dual-role post-own-propose with pending peer review → next-action MAY return `ack/nack`. Broad "dual-role" coverage IS named so the implementer can satisfy it without breaking AC, but reviewer_plan flagged this exact enumeration in their ACK to me — recommend slice-1 implement-phase to split this AC into the two sub-cases explicitly. Same applies to test cases in TASK-1-10. **Non-blocking** because reviewer_plan named it as a slice-1 enumeration request and TASK-1-10 already lists "dual-role-mid-review" so the implementer has a hook.

2. **R12 CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED filter — implicit, not positively asserted.** TASK-2-1 line 754-756 lists the six-event wait filter (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE/ACK/NACK + STATUS/RE_REVIEW/OVERSEER_ALERT). TASK-2-6 (i) line 873 snapshot-asserts "the six-event wait-filter set". Snapshot rigidity means adding a 7th event (CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED) would break the snapshot — so the invariant IS guarded, just implicitly via snapshot rigidity rather than via positive omission. **Non-blocking** because the snapshot test does catch the regression, but a one-line explicit assertion "assert 'CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED' not in wait_filter_argv" would be clearer documentation of the invariant and survives snapshot updates.

3. **R9 no-slice-case (EGG_SLICE_ID unset for plan/refine phases).** TASK-2-2 line 783 uses `${EGG_SLICE_ID:-}` shell substitution — emits empty string when unset. TASK-2-6 (iii) tests the populated path only. The orchestrator's gateway-side handling of an empty-string slice_id is unspecified — could be treated as valid scope (wrong) or rejected (right). **Non-blocking** because plan-phase pipelines typically run without slice scope today and the existing message-wait-loop handler at handlers/message.py:321 uses `resolve_slice_id(req)` which may return None — so the new wrapper behavior likely mirrors existing. Recommend TASK-2-6 add an "(viii) unset-slice case" test asserting either omission or explicit null payload — but the absence of this test is not blocking.

4. **R6 write-only fallback in TASK-3-2.** Lines 994-997 acceptance: "with EGG_BRC_MEMORY=write-only (slice-1 default), the prompt omits memory but still emits the git-log delta against the orchestrator's signal-level `changed_artifacts` as a fallback baseline". This documents the production-runtime intermediate state — without `last_reviewed_commit_sha` (which lives in memory), there's no SHA to anchor the git-log, so changed_artifacts fallback is used. **The full path IS exercised in unit tests under TASK-3-6 with mocked memory** (architect's commitment satisfied via unit-test surface). However, the production-runtime intermediate state in slice-3 has weakened adversarial re-review until slice-4's default flip. This is a documented limitation, not a blocker — slice-3's default-off flag means no production traffic uses this path; the unit-test surface IS where the architect committed. Recommend TASK-3-2 acceptance explicitly label this as "documented intermediate-state limitation resolved at slice-4 default-flip" rather than implying it's the intended permanent fallback. **Non-blocking**.

5. **Pre-emptive reviewer_plan suggestions.** reviewer_plan's ACK to me explicitly flagged R11 and R12 as needing slice-1 task AC enumeration. The task_planner's plan partially threads them but leaves explicit enumeration to implement phase (item 1 + 2 above). This is the same observation from a different lens — non-blocking, but worth knowing both risk_analyst and reviewer_plan converged on these as slice-1 AC tightening opportunities.

### Delta-bounded fresh review (mandate 2)
Audited the 1620-line plan as the first (and only) verdict cycle — no NACK history to anchor on. Applied risk-lens rubric: silent fallbacks (found R6 write-only fallback — non-blocking per above), runtime-primitive shape (TASK-2-1's reference to `egg-orch consensus confirmed` correctly matches the existing CLI at orch_cli.py:2753 — no orphan `progress complete`), atomicity assertions (TASK-1-9 covers tempfile + os.replace under fault injection), gateway-trust-boundary assumptions (TASK-2-4 wrapper-side gateway-session keep-alive uses lifecycle-secret-gated session refresh — same trust boundary), dual-role ordering (R11 under-specified but not absent), pre-confirm wait deadlock (R12 covered by snapshot rigidity). No issues found that wouldn't survive implement-phase pickup.

Verdict: ACK. The plan is implement-ready. All risk-register mitigations are concretely pinned in task ACs; the four non-blockers above are AC-tightening opportunities that the implement-phase coder/tester can realize without re-planning. The six-slice DAG, six required schema fields, atomic-write helper promotion, EGG_BRC_MEMORY mode matrix, EGG_BRC_EVENT_PUMP flag-gate, byte-for-byte legacy preservation, three-point WS7 measurement, R10 SDK-spawn-preserving e2e re-purposing, sandbox-image-rebuild-before-flag-flip, and 409-stale-version handling all map directly to specific task IDs with concrete acceptance criteria.

### [2026-06-01T04:06:12Z] reviewer_plan → task_planner (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for task_planner


Reviewed the 1,620-line plan against §1-§11. Ran the role↔files alignment validator (`parse_plan_file` + `validate_task_role_alignment` against `to_contract_slices()`) — 0 errors, so §8 structural alignment is clean. Independently re-verified plan-cited primitives: `consensus_wrapper.py:38/64-99/116/716`, `handlers/brc.py:505/586`, `handlers/message.py:267-429` heartbeat region, `routes/pipelines.py:12348 _build_brc_preamble` (callers :13659/13692/13720), `client.py:299-353` MCP registration block (EGG_MCP_TOOLS at :311 + :351), `peer_consensus.py:949-1024` _open_nacks_barrier_response, `orch_cli.py:3265/3485/3573/3600` argv prose-arg parsers, `kubernetes_spawner.py:332/333` job formats, `egg_overseer/state.py:266` atomic-write helper. The architect's 6-slice scaffold is correctly decomposed into ~36 tasks across coder/tester/documenter with clear acceptance criteria and matching dependencies. **One blocker** below, plus non-blockers.

### Blocking

1. **TASK-3-4 (and the architect-inherited modify-list) edits the WRONG mission.md path. The sandbox image does NOT bake-in `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` — it bakes `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`.** Independently verified:
   - `sandbox/Dockerfile:212-214`:
     ```
     RUN mkdir -p /opt/claude-rules
     COPY sandbox/claude-rules/*.md /opt/claude-rules/
     RUN chmod 644 /opt/claude-rules/*.md
     ```
   - `sandbox/entrypoint.py:967`: `_CLAUDE_RULES_DIR = Path("/opt/claude-rules")` — the agent's startup reads from the Dockerfile-baked location.
   - Two on-disk copies exist today (`sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` AND `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`), both 12,195 bytes, both byte-identical via `diff` returning empty, both tracked under the same git history. NO automated sync mechanism — searched `Makefile`, `scripts/`, `sandbox/scripts/`, `sandbox/Dockerfile`, `sandbox/entrypoint.py` for sync logic and found none. They're maintained as manual duplicates.
   
   TASK-3-4's `files:` list (plan line 1054) names ONLY `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md`. If a coder/documenter follows the task verbatim, the rewrite lands in the path that does NOT flow into the image. `sandbox/Dockerfile:213` copies the OLD `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` content into the new image. Deployed agents read the OLD content. The slice-4 spike (TASK-4-1) verifies image-tag freshness ("assert the sandbox image built in slice-3 is the deployed image tag") but does NOT verify mission.md CONTENT inside the image — so the spike passes mechanically while running with the pre-rewrite STAY-ALIVE/wait-loop bullets still in the agent's `/opt/claude-rules/mission.md`. The slice-4 flag flip happens, the new event-pump wrapper invokes agents that still see "never exit before the orchestrator stops you" guidance from the un-updated mission.md. Best case: agent confusion + non-deterministic behavior. Worst case: agent re-enters wait by following the still-present rule, replicating the #2906 fall-out the issue is designed to fix. Per §9: "If the primitive exists but in a different form than the plan assumes (different module, different signature, different scope — e.g. unit-test-only vs deployed-pod), NACK and quote the actual `file:line`." The primitive (mission.md as runtime-loaded rules) exists at a different deployed-pod path than the plan cites.
   
   **Fix — three valid options:**
   
   (a) **Edit BOTH paths in TASK-3-4** (simplest):
   ```yaml
   files:
     - sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md
     - sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md
   ```
   Acceptance criterion gets: "after the change, `diff sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` returns empty AND the new content is present in both files."
   
   (b) **Replace the cited path with `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` only** (cleaner if `agent-config/rules/` is dead-code / docs-only):
   ```yaml
   files:
     - sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md
   ```
   Add a separate task (or follow-up) to consolidate the duplicate paths — but that's scope creep this cycle should NOT take on.
   
   (c) **Add a slice-3 task to consolidate** the two paths (e.g. symlink one to the other, or pick one canonical and delete the other). Most invasive but addresses the underlying organizational tension.
   
   I recommend (a) — it's the lightest-weight fix that closes the gap and doesn't require resolving the broader `sandbox/agent-config/rules/` vs `sandbox/claude-rules/` choice now. Add a `// TODO #N` to consolidate later via separate issue.
   
   Additionally, **TASK-4-1 acceptance must be tightened to verify CONTENT, not just image tag.** Today's acceptance: "assert the sandbox image built in slice-3 ... is the deployed image tag — fail fast if the rebuild did not land." Add: "AND assert the deployed `/opt/claude-rules/mission.md` no longer contains the STAY-ALIVE / wait-loop bullet phrases the slice-3 task removed (specific phrase grep: `\\bSTAY-ALIVE\\b` and `wait-loop` should return zero matches in the file inside the pod)." Without this content assertion, the pre-flight passes on stale content + new image tag.

### Non-blocking

- **TASK-3-3 doesn't explicitly preserve the dual-mandate adversarial re-review banner at `routes/pipelines.py:12849-12872`.** TASK-3-3 description lists what to delete ("Producer Lifecycle step 4 wait-loop plumbing; Producer step 6 STAY-ALIVE loop; cursor / `--since` guidance") and what to keep ("agent roster, reviewer/producer assignments, dual-role ordering banner"). The dual-mandate re-review framing — "**This is adversarial re-review, not blocker-verification.** Your re-review has TWO equal-weight mandates..." — at lines 12849-12872 is Reviewer Lifecycle step 8 content, NOT seam-related (it's behavioral framing risk_analyst R6 anchors on). Architect's intent (per §design.runtime_primitive_assumptions) keeps non-seam content, but TASK-3-3 acceptance doesn't enumerate it. **Suggestion:** TASK-3-3 acceptance gets: "the dual-mandate re-review banner at routes/pipelines.py:12849-12872 (the `Your re-review has TWO equal-weight mandates...` block) is RETAINED in the collapsed preamble. Snapshot test asserts the phrase `Both mandates have equal weight` appears in the post-collapse preamble." Without this, the risk that drives risk_analyst R6 silently disappears.

- **TASK-3-4 assigns `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` to the documenter role; per #1537 these files are functional code (not docs).** Patterns at `shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py:243-247` exempt `sandbox/agent-config/rules/*.md` from the coder's docs-block precisely because "Agent config .md files are functional code (rules, skills, commands), not documentation. See #1537." The role↔files validator allows both coder and documenter to write the path (documenter's `**/*.md` allowed pattern; no specific block), so §8 doesn't error. But the conventional ownership per #1537 is CODER. **Suggestion:** TASK-3-4 role becomes `coder` (more aligned with #1537 spirit). If documenter is preferred for prose-rewriting reasons, document the deviation from #1537. Non-blocking because either role can technically push the file.

- **TASK-6-5 says "CLAUDE.md is at the project root — the documenter cannot push it but can stage edits under `.egg-state/agent-outputs/`."** This is INCORRECT. The documenter's allowed pattern includes `**/*.md` (DEFAULT_DOCS_GLOBS at `patterns.py:177-181` matches CLAUDE.md regardless of depth). I verified: `check_agent_file_access('documenter', ['CLAUDE.md'])` returns `(True, [], 'All files allowed for agent role')`. The "stage under .egg-state/agent-outputs/" workaround is unnecessary. **Suggestion:** TASK-6-5 description simplifies to "Update CLAUDE.md directly (documenter has `**/*.md` write permission)" with CLAUDE.md added to the `files:` list. Non-blocking because the workaround would still work; just adds unneeded friction.

- **TASK-6-6 latency comparison "vendored tarball of the deleted MCP code" is implementation-heavy and adds risk.** The task description says "the test fixture restores the deleted MCP code from a vendored tarball — implementation detail for the test" — this requires checking in the deleted MCP source as a test fixture, which is its own maintenance burden (the tarball gets stale). **Suggestion:** simplify to "capture the MCP-surface latency baseline DURING slice-5 (before slice-6's deletions land) and store it under `.egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json`. Slice-6 reads the stored baseline rather than reconstructing the MCP code." Same comparison, no vendored tarball, baseline is captured naturally during the BRC cycle progression. Non-blocking because the vendored-tarball approach works in principle.

- **od-6 Option B commitment (TASK-3-2) inherits the unmeasured per-event cache-invalidation risk I raised on the architect ACK.** Memory-at-tail-inline + distill-on-write rewrites the memory portion of the prompt per event. WS7 measurements were of a STABLE prefix; the per-event tail-mutation scenario is outside that envelope. TASK-3-7 captures WS7 measurement #1 (snapshot-time baseline) and TASK-4-1 captures WS7 measurement #2 (spike-time) — but neither isolates "same event-pump prefix + distinct memory tail" to validate Option B's cache assumption. **Suggestion:** TASK-3-7 (or a new TASK-3-8) captures a sub-measurement: run two events back-to-back with the same role + same prefix + memory rewritten between them; record `cache_read_input_tokens` on both. If the second event shows < 70% prefix cache_read (vs the WS7 baseline's ~92-99.99% on a stable prefix), surface as a HITL pause to consider Option C (net-new `--memory-file` flag at stable position). Non-blocking because TASK-4-1's wider measurement would eventually catch a large regression; the sub-measurement just makes it diagnostic earlier.

- **TASK-1-6 `EGG_BRC_MEMORY={off,write-only,full}` modes: slice-4 (TASK-4-2) skips the `write-only` mode and jumps straight from `off` → `full`.** This is fine for production rollout (write-only was a slice-1-internal staging mode) but means `write-only` mode is only exercised in tests. **Suggestion:** TASK-1-9 acceptance gets: "one test per mode (off, write-only, full) including the write-only-mode unique guarantee that writes happen but the `EGG_BRC_MEMORY=write-only` env produces ZERO reads." Non-blocking — current TASK-1-9 acceptance ("one test per `EGG_BRC_MEMORY` mode") already implies coverage.

- **TASK-2-6 (vii) "role_complete=true path calls `egg-orch consensus confirmed` and exits 0"** — good acceptance, but doesn't assert the wrapper does NOT also call `egg-orch progress complete` (the pseudocode-typo the architect corrected). **Suggestion:** add "(vii.b) wrapper does NOT invoke `egg-orch progress complete` on role_complete=true." Non-blocking; just defensive.

- **TASK-5-1 deprecation warning on argv is good, but TASK-6 doesn't follow through on deprecation.** Plan line 366-367: "Argv kept as fallback during transition (deprecation warning)." Slice-6 doesn't enforce removal. So argv prose acceptance is permanent after slice-6 — same observation I raised on architect's slice-6 goal. The deprecation warning is at least a signal. Non-blocking; follow-up issue tag would clean this up.

- **TASK-4-3 deletes `consensus_wrapper.py:418-449` SSE machinery.** I verified the SSE region: lines 407 (preamble comment), 424 (curl invocation), 458 (loop), 489-500 (consensus.reached detection + fallback). The 418-449 range slightly under-cites — the SSE block runs ~407-505. **Suggestion:** TASK-4-3 acceptance gets a `grep` assertion: "post-deletion, `grep -n 'consensus.reached\\|sse_url' orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` returns zero matches." Non-blocking; the description's intent is clear.

- **TASK-1-2 acceptance enumerates 7 derivation cases but doesn't explicitly include risk_analyst R11 (dual-role pre/post-propose) or R12 (pre-confirm-wait CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED filter set).** R11 is implicitly under "dual-role mid-review" but the specific "WORKING with peer producer CONSENSUS_PROPOSE pending → next-action MUST be 'propose', not 'review'" shape isn't named. R12 ("pre-confirm waits OMIT CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED; post-confirm waits INCLUDE it") doesn't map clearly to any of the 7 listed cases — it's a TASK-2-1 wrapper-side wait-construction concern, not a TASK-1-2 next-action concern, so this might be on the wrong slice. **Suggestion:** TASK-1-2 acceptance adds "dual-role WORKING + peer CONSENSUS_PROPOSE pending → next-action returns 'propose' (risk_analyst R11)"; TASK-2-1 acceptance gets "wait filter set is constructed from `consensus_status.is_role_confirmed` — pre-confirm OMITs CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED, post-confirm INCLUDES it (risk_analyst R12)." Non-blocking — risk_analyst raised these as mitigation specs.

Re-propose with TASK-3-4 updated to either (a) edit both `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` AND `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`, or (b) edit only `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` (the Dockerfile-baked canonical path). TASK-4-1 acceptance must add a CONTENT assertion (grep absence of "STAY-ALIVE" inside the deployed pod's `/opt/claude-rules/mission.md`), not just an image-tag freshness check. The non-blocking notes can be folded in if convenient.
