    \ `orch_cli.py:3265/3485/3573/3600` argv prose-arg parsers, `kubernetes_spawner.py:332/333`\
    \ job formats, `egg_overseer/state.py:266` atomic-write helper. The architect's\
    \ 6-slice scaffold is correctly decomposed into ~36 tasks across coder/tester/documenter\
    \ with clear acceptance criteria and matching dependencies. **One blocker** below,\
    \ plus non-blockers.\n\n### Blocking\n\n1. **TASK-3-4 (and the architect-inherited\
    \ modify-list) edits the WRONG mission.md path. The sandbox image does NOT bake-in\
    \ `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` \u2014 it bakes `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`.**\
    \ Independently verified:\n   - `sandbox/Dockerfile:212-214`:\n     ```\n    \
    \ RUN mkdir -p /opt/claude-rules\n     COPY sandbox/claude-rules/*.md /opt/claude-rules/\n\
    \     RUN chmod 644 /opt/claude-rules/*.md\n     ```\n   - `sandbox/entrypoint.py:967`:\
    \ `_CLAUDE_RULES_DIR = Path(\"/opt/claude-rules\")` \u2014 the agent's startup\
    \ reads from the Dockerfile-baked location.\n   - Two on-disk copies exist today\
    \ (`sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` AND `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`),\
    \ both 12,195 bytes, both byte-identical via `diff` returning empty, both tracked\
    \ under the same git history. NO automated sync mechanism \u2014 searched `Makefile`,\
    \ `scripts/`, `sandbox/scripts/`, `sandbox/Dockerfile`, `sandbox/entrypoint.py`\
    \ for sync logic and found none. They're maintained as manual duplicates.\n  \
    \ \n   TASK-3-4's `files:` list (plan line 1054) names ONLY `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md`.\
    \ If a coder/documenter follows the task verbatim, the rewrite lands in the path\
    \ that does NOT flow into the image. `sandbox/Dockerfile:213` copies the OLD `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`\
    \ content into the new image. Deployed agents read the OLD content. The slice-4\
    \ spike (TASK-4-1) verifies image-tag freshness (\"assert the sandbox image built\
    \ in slice-3 is the deployed image tag\") but does NOT verify mission.md CONTENT\
    \ inside the image \u2014 so the spike passes mechanically while running with\
    \ the pre-rewrite STAY-ALIVE/wait-loop bullets still in the agent's `/opt/claude-rules/mission.md`.\
    \ The slice-4 flag flip happens, the new event-pump wrapper invokes agents that\
    \ still see \"never exit before the orchestrator stops you\" guidance from the\
    \ un-updated mission.md. Best case: agent confusion + non-deterministic behavior.\
    \ Worst case: agent re-enters wait by following the still-present rule, replicating\
    \ the #2906 fall-out the issue is designed to fix. Per \xA79: \"If the primitive\
    \ exists but in a different form than the plan assumes (different module, different\
    \ signature, different scope \u2014 e.g. unit-test-only vs deployed-pod), NACK\
    \ and quote the actual `file:line`.\" The primitive (mission.md as runtime-loaded\
    \ rules) exists at a different deployed-pod path than the plan cites.\n   \n \
    \  **Fix \u2014 three valid options:**\n   \n   (a) **Edit BOTH paths in TASK-3-4**\
    \ (simplest):\n   ```yaml\n   files:\n     - sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md\n\
    \     - sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md\n   ```\n   Acceptance criterion gets:\
    \ \"after the change, `diff sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`\
    \ returns empty AND the new content is present in both files.\"\n   \n   (b) **Replace\
    \ the cited path with `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` only** (cleaner if `agent-config/rules/`\
    \ is dead-code / docs-only):\n   ```yaml\n   files:\n     - sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md\n\
    \   ```\n   Add a separate task (or follow-up) to consolidate the duplicate paths\
    \ \u2014 but that's scope creep this cycle should NOT take on.\n   \n   (c) **Add\
    \ a slice-3 task to consolidate** the two paths (e.g. symlink one to the other,\
    \ or pick one canonical and delete the other). Most invasive but addresses the\
    \ underlying organizational tension.\n   \n   I recommend (a) \u2014 it's the\
    \ lightest-weight fix that closes the gap and doesn't require resolving the broader\
    \ `sandbox/agent-config/rules/` vs `sandbox/claude-rules/` choice now. Add a `//\
    \ TODO #N` to consolidate later via separate issue.\n   \n   Additionally, **TASK-4-1\
    \ acceptance must be tightened to verify CONTENT, not just image tag.** Today's\
    \ acceptance: \"assert the sandbox image built in slice-3 ... is the deployed\
    \ image tag \u2014 fail fast if the rebuild did not land.\" Add: \"AND assert\
    \ the deployed `/opt/claude-rules/mission.md` no longer contains the STAY-ALIVE\
    \ / wait-loop bullet phrases the slice-3 task removed (specific phrase grep: `\\\
    \\bSTAY-ALIVE\\\\b` and `wait-loop` should return zero matches in the file inside\
    \ the pod).\" Without this content assertion, the pre-flight passes on stale content\
    \ + new image tag.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n- **TASK-3-3 doesn't explicitly preserve\
    \ the dual-mandate adversarial re-review banner at `routes/pipelines.py:12849-12872`.**\
    \ TASK-3-3 description lists what to delete (\"Producer Lifecycle step 4 wait-loop\
    \ plumbing; Producer step 6 STAY-ALIVE loop; cursor / `--since` guidance\") and\
    \ what to keep (\"agent roster, reviewer/producer assignments, dual-role ordering\
    \ banner\"). The dual-mandate re-review framing \u2014 \"**This is adversarial\
    \ re-review, not blocker-verification.** Your re-review has TWO equal-weight mandates...\"\
    \ \u2014 at lines 12849-12872 is Reviewer Lifecycle step 8 content, NOT seam-related\
    \ (it's behavioral framing risk_analyst R6 anchors on). Architect's intent (per\
    \ \xA7design.runtime_primitive_assumptions) keeps non-seam content, but TASK-3-3\
    \ acceptance doesn't enumerate it. **Suggestion:** TASK-3-3 acceptance gets: \"\
    the dual-mandate re-review banner at routes/pipelines.py:12849-12872 (the `Your\
    \ re-review has TWO equal-weight mandates...` block) is RETAINED in the collapsed\
    \ preamble. Snapshot test asserts the phrase `Both mandates have equal weight`\
    \ appears in the post-collapse preamble.\" Without this, the risk that drives\
    \ risk_analyst R6 silently disappears.\n\n- **TASK-3-4 assigns `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md`\
    \ to the documenter role; per #1537 these files are functional code (not docs).**\
    \ Patterns at `shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py:243-247` exempt `sandbox/agent-config/rules/*.md`\
    \ from the coder's docs-block precisely because \"Agent config .md files are functional\
    \ code (rules, skills, commands), not documentation. See #1537.\" The role\u2194\
    files validator allows both coder and documenter to write the path (documenter's\
    \ `**/*.md` allowed pattern; no specific block), so \xA78 doesn't error. But the\
    \ conventional ownership per #1537 is CODER. **Suggestion:** TASK-3-4 role becomes\
    \ `coder` (more aligned with #1537 spirit). If documenter is preferred for prose-rewriting\
    \ reasons, document the deviation from #1537. Non-blocking because either role\
    \ can technically push the file.\n\n- **TASK-6-5 says \"CLAUDE.md is at the project\
    \ root \u2014 the documenter cannot push it but can stage edits under `.egg-state/agent-outputs/`.\"\
    ** This is INCORRECT. The documenter's allowed pattern includes `**/*.md` (DEFAULT_DOCS_GLOBS\
    \ at `patterns.py:177-181` matches CLAUDE.md regardless of depth). I verified:\
    \ `check_agent_file_access('documenter', ['CLAUDE.md'])` returns `(True, [], 'All\
    \ files allowed for agent role')`. The \"stage under .egg-state/agent-outputs/\"\
    \ workaround is unnecessary. **Suggestion:** TASK-6-5 description simplifies to\
    \ \"Update CLAUDE.md directly (documenter has `**/*.md` write permission)\" with\
    \ CLAUDE.md added to the `files:` list. Non-blocking because the workaround would\
    \ still work; just adds unneeded friction.\n\n- **TASK-6-6 latency comparison\
    \ \"vendored tarball of the deleted MCP code\" is implementation-heavy and adds\
    \ risk.** The task description says \"the test fixture restores the deleted MCP\
    \ code from a vendored tarball \u2014 implementation detail for the test\" \u2014\
    \ this requires checking in the deleted MCP source as a test fixture, which is\
    \ its own maintenance burden (the tarball gets stale). **Suggestion:** simplify\
    \ to \"capture the MCP-surface latency baseline DURING slice-5 (before slice-6's\
    \ deletions land) and store it under `.egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json`.\
    \ Slice-6 reads the stored baseline rather than reconstructing the MCP code.\"\
    \ Same comparison, no vendored tarball, baseline is captured naturally during\
    \ the BRC cycle progression. Non-blocking because the vendored-tarball approach\
    \ works in principle.\n\n- **od-6 Option B commitment (TASK-3-2) inherits the\
    \ unmeasured per-event cache-invalidation risk I raised on the architect ACK.**\
    \ Memory-at-tail-inline + distill-on-write rewrites the memory portion of the\
    \ prompt per event. WS7 measurements were of a STABLE prefix; the per-event tail-mutation\
    \ scenario is outside that envelope. TASK-3-7 captures WS7 measurement #1 (snapshot-time\
    \ baseline) and TASK-4-1 captures WS7 measurement #2 (spike-time) \u2014 but neither\
    \ isolates \"same event-pump prefix + distinct memory tail\" to validate Option\
    \ B's cache assumption. **Suggestion:** TASK-3-7 (or a new TASK-3-8) captures\
    \ a sub-measurement: run two events back-to-back with the same role + same prefix\
    \ + memory rewritten between them; record `cache_read_input_tokens` on both. If\
    \ the second event shows < 70% prefix cache_read (vs the WS7 baseline's ~92-99.99%\
    \ on a stable prefix), surface as a HITL pause to consider Option C (net-new `--memory-file`\
    \ flag at stable position). Non-blocking because TASK-4-1's wider measurement\
    \ would eventually catch a large regression; the sub-measurement just makes it\
    \ diagnostic earlier.\n\n- **TASK-1-6 `EGG_BRC_MEMORY={off,write-only,full}` modes:\
    \ slice-4 (TASK-4-2) skips the `write-only` mode and jumps straight from `off`\
    \ \u2192 `full`.** This is fine for production rollout (write-only was a slice-1-internal\
    \ staging mode) but means `write-only` mode is only exercised in tests. **Suggestion:**\
    \ TASK-1-9 acceptance gets: \"one test per mode (off, write-only, full) including\
    \ the write-only-mode unique guarantee that writes happen but the `EGG_BRC_MEMORY=write-only`\
    \ env produces ZERO reads.\" Non-blocking \u2014 current TASK-1-9 acceptance (\"\
    one test per `EGG_BRC_MEMORY` mode\") already implies coverage.\n\n- **TASK-2-6\
    \ (vii) \"role_complete=true path calls `egg-orch consensus confirmed` and exits\
    \ 0\"** \u2014 good acceptance, but doesn't assert the wrapper does NOT also call\
    \ `egg-orch progress complete` (the pseudocode-typo the architect corrected).\
    \ **Suggestion:** add \"(vii.b) wrapper does NOT invoke `egg-orch progress complete`\
    \ on role_complete=true.\" Non-blocking; just defensive.\n\n- **TASK-5-1 deprecation\
    \ warning on argv is good, but TASK-6 doesn't follow through on deprecation.**\
    \ Plan line 366-367: \"Argv kept as fallback during transition (deprecation warning).\"\
    \ Slice-6 doesn't enforce removal. So argv prose acceptance is permanent after\
    \ slice-6 \u2014 same observation I raised on architect's slice-6 goal. The deprecation\
    \ warning is at least a signal. Non-blocking; follow-up issue tag would clean\
    \ this up.\n\n- **TASK-4-3 deletes `consensus_wrapper.py:418-449` SSE machinery.**\
    \ I verified the SSE region: lines 407 (preamble comment), 424 (curl invocation),\
    \ 458 (loop), 489-500 (consensus.reached detection + fallback). The 418-449 range\
    \ slightly under-cites \u2014 the SSE block runs ~407-505. **Suggestion:** TASK-4-3\
    \ acceptance gets a `grep` assertion: \"post-deletion, `grep -n 'consensus.reached\\\
    \\|sse_url' orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` returns zero matches.\" Non-blocking;\
    \ the description's intent is clear.\n\n- **TASK-1-2 acceptance enumerates 7 derivation\
    \ cases but doesn't explicitly include risk_analyst R11 (dual-role pre/post-propose)\
    \ or R12 (pre-confirm-wait CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED filter set).** R11 is implicitly\
    \ under \"dual-role mid-review\" but the specific \"WORKING with peer producer\
    \ CONSENSUS_PROPOSE pending \u2192 next-action MUST be 'propose', not 'review'\"\
    \ shape isn't named. R12 (\"pre-confirm waits OMIT CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED; post-confirm\
    \ waits INCLUDE it\") doesn't map clearly to any of the 7 listed cases \u2014\
    \ it's a TASK-2-1 wrapper-side wait-construction concern, not a TASK-1-2 next-action\
    \ concern, so this might be on the wrong slice. **Suggestion:** TASK-1-2 acceptance\
    \ adds \"dual-role WORKING + peer CONSENSUS_PROPOSE pending \u2192 next-action\
    \ returns 'propose' (risk_analyst R11)\"; TASK-2-1 acceptance gets \"wait filter\
    \ set is constructed from `consensus_status.is_role_confirmed` \u2014 pre-confirm\
    \ OMITs CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED, post-confirm INCLUDES it (risk_analyst R12).\" Non-blocking\
    \ \u2014 risk_analyst raised these as mitigation specs.\n\nRe-propose with TASK-3-4\
    \ updated to either (a) edit both `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` AND\
    \ `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`, or (b) edit only `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`\
    \ (the Dockerfile-baked canonical path). TASK-4-1 acceptance must add a CONTENT\
    \ assertion (grep absence of \"STAY-ALIVE\" inside the deployed pod's `/opt/claude-rules/mission.md`),\
    \ not just an image-tag freshness check. The non-blocking notes can be folded\
    \ in if convenient.\n"
  revision_count: 1

Blocker fix: TASK-3-4 edits BOTH `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` AND `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` (the Dockerfile-baked runtime path per sandbox/Dockerfile:212-214 + sandbox/entrypoint.py:967 — independently verified the duplicate path); acceptance asserts `diff` returns empty and `rg 'STAY-ALIVE\b|wait-loop|never exit'` returns zero matches in both files. Splitting into two role-tasks would double BRC review surface for one rewrite. TASK-4-1 spike pre-flight tightened: in addition to image-tag freshness, exec into deployed pod and `grep -E 'STAY-ALIVE\b|wait-loop|never exit' /opt/claude-rules/mission.md` must return zero matches — catches stale content + fresh tag. Non-blockers folded in: TASK-3-3 preserves dual-mandate adversarial re-review banner at pipelines.py:12849-12872 with snapshot phrase assertion; TASK-1-2/1-10 split dual-role into R11 sub-cases a/b (9 derivation cases now); TASK-2-1 wait-filter constructed conditionally on consensus_status.is_role_confirmed per R12; TASK-2-6 adds vii.b/viii/ix defensive tests (no `progress complete` call, conditional CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED, unset-EGG_SLICE_ID); TASK-4-3 grep assertion for SSE / recovery-prompt / restart-cap strings; TASK-6-5 drops CLAUDE.md staging workaround (documenter has direct write); TASK-6-6 baseline capture moved OUT of vendored-tarball fixture INTO new slice-5 TASK-5-7 (.egg-state/agent-outputs/latency-mcp-baseline.json captured on still-live MCP surface); TASK-1-9 write-only-mode test made explicit (writes happen, ZERO reads via fixture spy). 43 tasks across 6 slices.

**Adversarial re-review**

**Your v2 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v1 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the delta since your last review (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md: `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` — the commits landed since the version you last verdicted, NOT the whole accumulated proposal surface) as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v1 NACK history. Read that diff as if you'd never seen the prior version. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied. **Mandate 2 is bounded to this delta** — it does NOT ask you to re-traverse the whole accumulated surface from earlier cycles; that work was amortized when you first reviewed those commits.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has newly-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has newly-introduced races, contract has newly-introduced AC drift, code has newly-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did prior issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does this delta introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, this delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v2 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from that diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v2 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v1 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.

- AC asserts `diff` returns empty between the two paths AND `rg 'STAY-ALIVE\b|wait-loop|never exit'` returns zero matches in BOTH files.
- TASK-4-1 spike pre-flight tightened: kubectl-exec into deployed pod + `grep -E 'STAY-ALIVE\b|wait-loop|never exit' /opt/claude-rules/mission.md` returns zero. Catches the failure modes (fresh tag + stale content, or fresh content + stale tag).
- Role correctly kept as `documenter` with file-restriction verification: `shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py:177-181` DEFAULT_DOCS_GLOBS `**/*.md` covers both paths; coder is blocked from `sandbox/claude-rules/*.md` (no `#1537`-style exemption for that directory). Single-documenter task is the lighter resolution.

**My v1 non-blockers (R11, R12, R9 unset-slice, latency-baseline architecture):** ALL FOLDED IN.
- **R11 dual-role split:** TASK-1-2 now lists 9 derivation cases (was 7), with (3) "dual-role WORKING + peer CONSENSUS_PROPOSE pending → next-action returns `propose` per #2749" and (4) "dual-role post-own-propose with pending peer review → returns `ack`/`nack`" as separate sub-cases. TASK-1-10 mirrors with "two dual-role sub-cases are distinct named tests (NOT collapsed into one)".
- **R12 CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED filter:** TASK-2-1 acceptance now: "wait-filter set is constructed conditionally from `consensus_status.is_role_confirmed` — pre-confirm waits OMIT `CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED`, post-confirm STAY-ALIVE waits INCLUDE it". TASK-2-6 (viii) tests both branches.
- **R9 unset-EGG_SLICE_ID:** TASK-2-6 (ix) now tests "unset-`EGG_SLICE_ID` case (plan/refine phase) emits either explicit-null or omitted slice_id on the heartbeat payload (NOT empty-string)".
- **Defensive grep for `progress complete` typo (TASK-2-6 vii.b):** `rg 'progress complete'` against emitted bash returns zero matches. Guards against the architect-corrected pseudocode typo.
- **Defensive grep for partial deletion (TASK-4-3):** `rg 'consensus\.reached|sse_url|_RECOVERY_SYSTEM_PROMPT|MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS'` returns zero matches post-deletion.

- **TASK-3-3** explicitly preserves the dual-mandate adversarial re-review banner at `pipelines.py:12849-12872` with snapshot phrase assertion "Both mandates have equal weight". This is the behavioural framing the orchestrator injects into every CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW message — protecting it from accidental collapse is essential for the adversarial-re-review enforcement that all v2 verdicts (including this one) thread through.
- **TASK-6-5** correctly drops the `.egg-state/agent-outputs/`-staging workaround for CLAUDE.md (documenter has direct `**/*.md` write access); CLAUDE.md added directly to the files list.
- **TASK-1-9** memory mode test rigor: "off produces zero file touches; write-only produces writes but ZERO reads — the read code path is exercised through a mocked-fixture spy that asserts no read calls happen; full enables both". Spy assertion is stronger than just "doesn't read in write-only mode".

### Mandate 2 — Fresh delta audit (`d9b1589ec..d21daaed7` as if no v1 history)

Risk-lens rubric applied bounded to the new hunks:
- **Silent-fallback shapes:** Looked for new fallback paths that silently weaken adversarial-review. None introduced in this delta — the only fallback is the pre-existing write-only `changed_artifacts` fallback (TASK-3-2 unchanged from v1; was already non-blocker).
- **Shell-command executability in ACs:** `diff` is standard, `rg 'STAY-ALIVE\b'` works (rg/PCRE supports `\b`), `grep -E '...\b'` works under GNU grep (Linux pod default), `kubectl exec` into running pod is standard local_pipeline pattern. The defensive grep `rg 'progress complete'` is broader than needed (could match the literal `progress complete` in a comment or unrelated message body) — minor nit, would prefer `rg 'egg-orch progress complete'` for narrower match, but not blocking because slice-2's wrapper template is small and a false positive is easy to debug.
- **File-restriction shape under role changes:** TASK-3-4 documenter role verified at `patterns.py:177-181` (DEFAULT_DOCS_GLOBS `**/*.md`). TASK-3-4 includes `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` in files list; documenter can write it because the `**/*.md` glob is in the role's allowed_patterns, not blocked by any specific block_pattern for that path. ✅
- **Atomicity / fault-injection:** TASK-1-9 atomic-write under fault injection is unchanged from v1. ✅
- **Doc-snippet executability:** `diff sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` would execute as written. `grep -E '...' /opt/claude-rules/mission.md` would execute as written inside a kubectl-exec context. ✅
- **API/identifier shape:** `consensus_status.is_role_confirmed` is the wrapper-derived field name (the JSON path is `consensus.agents.<role>.confirmed` per `mcp__brc__get_state` output shape) — acceptable conceptual specification; implementer derives. ✅
- **`/opt/claude-rules/mission.md` content-grep:** correctly anchors on the Dockerfile-baked runtime path, not the documentation duplicate. ✅
- **TASK-5-7 baseline JSON commit pattern:** committed measurement artifact at `.egg-state/agent-outputs/` — tester role write access via `patterns.py` `tester:266-287`. ✅. The baseline is captured once; subsequent re-runs of slice-5 would not re-capture (and TASK-5-7 acceptance doesn't require re-capture). Acceptable for a one-shot comparison test.
- **Race shape — concurrent re-runs of slice-5 TASK-5-7 + slice-6 TASK-6-6:** the baseline JSON is read by slice-6 from the committed git state, not from a live capture, so there's no race. ✅
- **Wait-filter conditional construction edge case:** What about CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW post-confirm? Both pre-confirm and post-confirm waits should include it (re-review can fire at any time). The v2 conditional text only discusses CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED inclusion — but the architect's design pseudocode lists RE_REVIEW in both filter sets, and the implementer would carry that through. **Mild gap:** TASK-2-1 could explicitly enumerate "CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW always included regardless of confirm state" but doesn't. Non-blocking — the snapshot tests in TASK-2-6 would catch any regression, and the architect's commitment is unchanged.

### Specific shapes I CHECKED and did NOT find issues with
- silent fallback weakening adversarial review
- shell-command syntax errors in `rg` / `grep` / `diff` assertions
- file-restriction violations from role changes
- atomicity contracts surviving fault injection
- doc-snippet executability (commands would run as written)
- API/identifier shape drift relative to existing orchestrator
- runtime-path correctness for `mission.md`
- baseline-JSON capture/read race
- conditional wait-filter construction edge cases (CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW always-on)

### Non-blocking notes (no fix expected)
1. TASK-2-6 (vii.b) `rg 'progress complete'` could be tightened to `rg 'egg-orch progress complete'` for narrower match.
2. TASK-2-1 conditional wait-filter spec could explicitly call out "CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW always included" alongside the CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED conditional inclusion — implementer would carry this through anyway, but explicitness avoids accidental omission during snapshot updates.

Verdict: ACK v2. Plan is implement-ready. Reviewer_plan blocker addressed with a more robust solution than the minimum (dual-path edit + content-grep pre-flight). All risk-lens non-blockers from my v1 ACK folded in with strong test rigor. Mandate-2 delta audit surfaces no new blockers.       \ write-only produces writes but ZERO reads \u2014 the read code path is exercised\
      \ through a mocked-fixture spy that asserts no read calls happen; full enables\
      \ both\". Spy assertion is stronger than just \"doesn't read in write-only mode\"\
      .\n\n### Mandate 2 \u2014 Fresh delta audit (`d9b1589ec..d21daaed7` as if no\
      \ v1 history)\n\nRisk-lens rubric applied bounded to the new hunks:\n- **Silent-fallback\

      \ non-blocker).\n- **Shell-command executability in ACs:** `diff` is standard,\
      \ `rg 'STAY-ALIVE\\b'` works (rg/PCRE supports `\\b`), `grep -E '...\\b'` works\
      \ under GNU grep (Linux pod default), `kubectl exec` into running pod is standard\
      \ local_pipeline pattern. The defensive grep `rg 'progress complete'` is broader\
      \ than needed (could match the literal `progress complete` in a comment or unrelated\
      \ message body) \u2014 minor nit, would prefer `rg 'egg-orch progress complete'`\
      \ for narrower match, but not blocking because slice-2's wrapper template is\
      \ small and a false positive is easy to debug.\n- **File-restriction shape under\

      \ in files list; documenter can write it because the `**/*.md` glob is in the\
      \ role's allowed_patterns, not blocked by any specific block_pattern for that\
      \ path. \u2705\n- **Atomicity / fault-injection:** TASK-1-9 atomic-write under\
      \ fault injection is unchanged from v1. \u2705\n- **Doc-snippet executability:**\
      \ `diff sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md`\
      \ would execute as written. `grep -E '...' /opt/claude-rules/mission.md` would\
      \ execute as written inside a kubectl-exec context. \u2705\n- **API/identifier\
      \ shape:** `consensus_status.is_role_confirmed` is the wrapper-derived field\

      \ output shape) \u2014 acceptable conceptual specification; implementer derives.\
      \ \u2705\n- **`/opt/claude-rules/mission.md` content-grep:** correctly anchors\
      \ on the Dockerfile-baked runtime path, not the documentation duplicate. \u2705\
      \n- **TASK-5-7 baseline JSON commit pattern:** committed measurement artifact\

      \ Acceptable for a one-shot comparison test.\n- **Race shape \u2014 concurrent\
      \ re-runs of slice-5 TASK-5-7 + slice-6 TASK-6-6:** the baseline JSON is read\
      \ by slice-6 from the committed git state, not from a live capture, so there's\

### [2026-06-01T04:20:52Z] reviewer_plan → task_planner (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for task_planner


Reviewed v1→v2 delta as a fresh reviewer per dual-mandate framing. **Mandate 1 (v1 blockers): PASSES.** TASK-3-4 now lists both `sandbox/agent-config/rules/mission.md` and `sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md` with `diff` + content-grep acceptance; TASK-4-1 acceptance adds `kubectl exec` + content-grep into `/opt/claude-rules/mission.md`; the role-as-documenter rationale is correct (independently verified — `check_agent_file_access('coder', ['sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md'])` returns `(False, ['sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md'], "Agent role 'coder' cannot modify: sandbox/claude-rules/mission.md")` while documenter is allowed via `**/*.md`); TASK-3-3 dual-mandate banner preservation added; TASK-6-5 CLAUDE.md staging workaround dropped; TASK-6-6 vendored-tarball replaced with slice-5 baseline capture; TASK-1-2/1-10 now enumerate R11 sub-cases; TASK-2-1 R12 wait-filter conditional; TASK-2-6 defensive (vii.b/viii/ix) tests; TASK-4-3 grep assertion for partial-deletion; TASK-1-9 write-only mode spy. Solid v1-blocker resolution.

**Mandate 2 (fresh-reviewer audit of v1→v2 delta): FAILS — multiple §9 primitive-existence violations found in the new TASK-4-1/5-7/6-6/1-10 hunks.** Specific shapes I audited: doc-snippet executability (would a coder following TASK-4-1 verbatim be able to import the cited fixtures?), API-deprecation (the snapshot phrase assertion in TASK-3-3 actually matches the source?), silent-fallback shapes, primitive existence in newly-introduced text. All of these were applied to ONLY the v1→v2 diff (`git diff d9b1589ec..d21daaed7`).

### Blocking

1. **§9 — `integration_tests/local_pipeline/` directory does NOT EXIST in this codebase, yet TASK-4-1 / TASK-5-7 / TASK-6-6 all create files under it and reference fixtures from a non-existent conftest.** Verified verbatim:
   - `find . -type d -name "local_pipeline" -not -path "*/.venv/*"` returns **zero hits** (no such directory).
   - `find . -name "local_pipeline_stack*"` returns **zero hits** (no such fixture).
   - Actual integration-test layout: `ls integration_tests/` → `__init__.py  conftest.py  epic_pipeline/  regression/  sdlc/  test_credential_security.py  test_deployment_validation_logic.py  test_error_recovery.py  test_fail_closed.py  test_gateway_*.py  test_k8s_*.py  test_network_*.py  test_orchestrator_mcp_contract.py  test_per_agent_enforcement.py  test_performance.py  test_policy_enforcement.py  test_rate_limiting.py  test_sandbox_mcp_tools_e2e.py  test_sdk_interception.py  test_slice_pipeline_e2e.py  test_stack_lifecycle.py`. No `local_pipeline/`.
   - The actual `gateway_url` fixture is at **`integration_tests/conftest.py:225`** (`gateway_url = f"http://{gateway_ip}:{gateway_port_str}"`) — not `integration_tests/local_pipeline/conftest.py:261`. The fixture is yielded by `_k8s_egg_stack()` at integration_tests/conftest.py:172 and exposed via `egg_stack: EggStack` (line 340).
   - The actual session-scoped k3s fixture is **`egg_stack`** at `integration_tests/conftest.py:340`, not `local_pipeline_stack`.
   
   The plan's `Trust-boundary scope (#10)` section (lines 158-162) explicitly cites: `"integration_tests/local_pipeline/conftest.py:261 is the only gateway_url pytest fixture; kubectl-gated via local_pipeline_stack"`. Both citations are wrong; the directory doesn't exist and the gating fixture has a different name. TASK-4-1 description (plan line 1147-1149) writes: `"under local_pipeline/; the fixture gateway_url from integration_tests/local_pipeline/conftest.py:261 is the only one usable here"` — the fixture cannot be imported because the file doesn't exist. TASK-5-7 creates `integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_mcp_baseline_capture.py` and TASK-6-6 creates `integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_mcp_to_cli_latency.py` — both at the non-existent path.
   
   **Coder implementing TASK-4-1 verbatim would hit a pytest collection error** at the first `def test_…(gateway_url):` — pytest resolves fixtures from the nearest conftest upward, and there's no conftest under `integration_tests/local_pipeline/`. The test would never run, the spike acceptance criteria a-f are never verified, and slice-4 silently passes the wrong gate.
   
   **Fix — three valid options:**
   
   (a) **Re-home all three tests under existing fixture scope.** Move TASK-4-1/5-7/6-6 to `integration_tests/test_event_pump_spike_2906.py` etc. — directly under `integration_tests/`, where `egg_stack` and the underlying `gateway_url` (it's actually `egg_stack.gateway_url`, an attribute on the dataclass — see `integration_tests/conftest.py:78 gateway_url: str`) are already wired through the parent conftest. Update the citations in the plan's primitive table accordingly. This is the lightest-weight fix.
   
   (b) **Create `integration_tests/local_pipeline/conftest.py` as part of slice-2 or slice-4 (NEW — task TASK-X-Y).** Stand up the new fixture surface with its own `gateway_url` shim over the parent `egg_stack.gateway_url`. Adds an unnecessary level of indirection but matches the architect's mental model.
   
   (c) **Use the existing `integration_tests/regression/` tier**, which already drives in-process BRC against real Flask blueprints (per its conftest.py docstring). But this only works for in-process verification — for k3s spike work (TASK-4-1) you need real agent pods, which is the `egg_stack` path.
   
   I recommend (a). Re-cite the actual primitives:
   - Use `egg_stack: EggStack` (session-scoped, `integration_tests/conftest.py:340`).
   - For the gateway URL, use `egg_stack.gateway_url` (attribute on the dataclass at `integration_tests/conftest.py:78`).
   - For the orchestrator URL, use `egg_stack.orchestrator_url` (via the `orchestrator_url` fixture at `integration_tests/conftest.py:357`).
   - For lifecycle secret, use `egg_stack.lifecycle_secret` (via `lifecycle_secret` fixture at `:363`).

2. **§9 — `ScriptedProvider` is NOT a class that exists in this codebase. TASK-5-7 and TASK-1-10 invoke it as if it were a runnable test fixture.** Verified verbatim:
   - `grep -rn "class ScriptedProvider" --include="*.py" .` returns **zero hits**.
   - `find . -name "ScriptedProvider*"` returns **zero hits**.
   - The string `ScriptedProvider` appears only in: (i) **comments inside `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_*.py:18-20` explicitly stating that it CANNOT be used** ("ScriptedProvider can't drive deployed agent pods (see #2474)"), (ii) **prompt-building text** inside `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:4873/4882/4917/4943/4976/14125/14349/14488` (the reviewer / planner prompts that themselves WARN about ScriptedProvider as a §9/§10 example), and (iii) `orchestrator/tests/test_pipeline_prompts.py:5702` asserting the word appears in the prompt.
   - The integration_tests/regression/conftest.py:45 explicitly says: `"#2474 recommends after the ScriptedProvider pod-injection avenue was ruled out"` — i.e., the pod-injection approach was abandoned; no ScriptedProvider primitive was built.
   
   TASK-5-7 description writes: `"drives a 5-role ScriptedProvider consensus to CONFIRMED on the still-live MCP surface"` — there is no ScriptedProvider to drive a consensus with. TASK-1-10 description writes: `"using ScriptedProvider-driven fixtures from orchestrator/tests/conftest.py"` — but `head -50 orchestrator/tests/conftest.py` returns only sys.path setup + lifecycle-secret + docker import; **no ScriptedProvider fixtures exist there**. (I verified the entire conftest.py contains no `ScriptedProvider` or `scripted_provider` string.)
   
   This is the EXACT pattern §9 names with a specific example: `"| ScriptedProvider | class | grep -rn 'class ScriptedProvider' sandbox/ k8s/ orchestrator/ | 0 hits → NACK |"`. The plan itself reproduces this anti-pattern.
   
   **Fix — two options:**
   
   (a) **Drop the ScriptedProvider references.** TASK-1-10 should use whatever shape `orchestrator/tests/conftest.py` actually exposes for unit-testing the `consensus next-action` route (likely just FlaskClient + lifecycle_secret + in-process `PeerConsensusTracker` per the orchestrator-side BRC test pattern at `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py`). TASK-5-7 should drop ScriptedProvider entirely — the baseline-capture strategy needs a real agent pod tier (under integration_tests/, using `egg_stack`) or a microbenchmark of in-process MCP dispatch vs subprocess CLI invocation, NOT a non-existent ScriptedProvider consensus.
   
   (b) **If "ScriptedProvider" is intended as a placeholder for a yet-to-be-built primitive,** the plan must mark it `(NEW — task TASK-X-Y)` per §9's exception clause, and a creating task must produce it. The plan as written does not introduce this primitive in any task. (And #2474 already ruled out pod-injection ScriptedProvider, so a NEW one would re-litigate that decision.)
   
   I recommend (a). For TASK-5-7's baseline-capture specifically: capture latency on a real `egg_stack`-driven 5-role consensus with the MCP surface live (slice-5 is additive — MCP is still registered). The agents use real Claude/Qwen LLMs (not ScriptedProvider). Slice-6's TASK-6-6 runs the same consensus shape with MCP deleted. Both measurements use the SAME real-agent path; the only delta is the tool surface. This is the methodology that actually validates the < 5% latency budget.

3. **§9 — TASK-3-3 snapshot assertion uses a phrase that is NOT in the cited preamble range.** Verified verbatim:
   - `grep -n "Both mandates have equal weight" orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` returns `13292:        "Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new "` — **at line 13292, NOT in the 12849-12872 range**.
   - The phrase at line 13292 is in `_build_adversarial_reprime` (a separately-injected function for `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW` triggers), NOT in `_build_brc_preamble` (which starts at line 12348 and ends well before 13200).
   - The 12849-12872 range that TASK-3-3 cites preserves DIFFERENT phrases. Reading lines 12849-12872 directly: `"This is adversarial re-review, not blocker-verification."` (12849), `"Your re-review has TWO equal-weight mandates: (1) verify the blockers from your prior NACK were addressed AND (2) audit the delta..."` (12850-), `"Both must pass to ACK."` (12856-12857), `"find ALL issues, last line of defense before production"`, `"Re-reviews are cheap by design"`, `"NACK without hesitance"`.
   
   TASK-3-3 acceptance (plan line 1058-1060) writes: `"the phrase 'Both mandates have equal weight' appears in the post-collapse preamble snapshot"`. The phrase is NOT in the source range being preserved. The snapshot test as specified would FAIL (the phrase doesn't appear in `_build_brc_preamble`'s output).
   
   **Fix:** Replace the asserted phrase with one that IS in lines 12849-12872, e.g. `"Both must pass to ACK"` (line 12856-12857) or `"adversarial re-review, not blocker-verification"` (line 12849) or `"TWO equal-weight mandates"` (line 12850). Recommend `"Both must pass to ACK"` because it's the most specific to the dual-mandate framing and unlikely to appear elsewhere by coincidence.

### Non-blocking

- **TASK-5-7 "5-role ScriptedProvider consensus" framing also conflates two test tiers.** Even setting aside that ScriptedProvider doesn't exist as a class — if the intent is "an integration test that captures latency on real agents using a substitute LLM," ScriptedProvider isn't the term for it. The "egg-pilot" / litellm-routed LLM substitution is at a different layer than the orchestrator-side ScriptedProvider double the BRC concurrency tests reference. Pick the right level: real LLM (Claude / Qwen) in real pods, or the orchestrator-Python-API consensus pattern at `integration_tests/regression/test_brc_concurrency.py`. The plan's text reads as if both apply, but they're disjoint test surfaces. Once issue 2 above is fixed, this clarifies itself.

- **`Test strategy` (lines 191-216) and `Manual pre/post-merge steps` (lines 233-265) inherit the `local_pipeline_stack` / `integration_tests/local_pipeline/` references.** Fixing issue 1 above also requires updating these prose sections to cite the correct fixture (`egg_stack`) and path (`integration_tests/`).

- **TASK-5-7 description "writes per-event wall-clock samples (event-type, start_ts, end_ts, agent-process exit code)" presumes the test spawns real agent processes.** If it does (via `egg_stack` after fix 1), the "agent-process exit code" is meaningful. If it doesn't (in-process ScriptedProvider as currently described), there's no exit code to capture. The fix to issue 2 should also clarify which tier this is.

- **TASK-3-3 byte-size drop ≥ 40% target is ambitious.** The preamble is large (`_build_brc_preamble` runs from line 12348 to well past 13000) but the seam-related text task_planner targets (Producer Lifecycle step 4 wait-loop plumbing; Producer step 6 STAY-ALIVE loop; cursor / `--since` guidance) is a subset of that. Without measuring before commitment, 40% may be unreachable. **Suggestion:** soften to "≥ 25%" (the exact number is the snapshot baseline result, not a pre-set target).

- **TASK-1-10 dual-role sub-case naming**: The acceptance criterion enforces "the two dual-role sub-cases are distinct named tests (NOT collapsed into one)". This is good, but the names (R11 sub-case a / sub-case b) reference a risk_analyst output that lives in the .egg-state. The test names should be self-describing in code: e.g. `test_next_action_dual_role_pre_propose_returns_propose` and `test_next_action_dual_role_post_propose_returns_review`. **Suggestion:** include the recommended test names in the acceptance criterion.

(3) TASK-3-3 phrase "Both mandates have equal weight" lives at pipelines.py:13292 inside `_build_adversarial_reprime`, not inside `_build_brc_preamble`'s 12849-12872 range; replaced with "Both must pass to ACK" (verified at 12856-12857 — correctly inside the cited preamble range). **Adversarial re-review**

**Your v3 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v2 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the delta since your last review (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md: `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` — the commits landed since the version you last verdicted, NOT the whole accumulated proposal surface) as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v2 NACK history. Read that diff as if you'd never seen the prior version. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied. **Mandate 2 is bounded to this delta** — it does NOT ask you to re-traverse the whole accumulated surface from earlier cycles; that work was amortized when you first reviewed those commits.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has newly-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has newly-introduced races, contract has newly-introduced AC drift, code has newly-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did prior issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does this delta introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, this delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v3 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from that diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v3 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v2 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.

Adversarial re-review of task_planner v3 (`d21daaed7..b14ca3069`, +155/-96 in plan.md). Both mandates pass; ACK.

### Self-acknowledgment of a risk-analyst miss
Reviewer_plan v2 caught three runtime-primitive mismatches in task_planner v2 that I should have flagged per #2594 in my own v1/v2 ACKs:
- `integration_tests/local_pipeline/` directory does NOT exist.
- `class ScriptedProvider` does NOT exist in this codebase (ruled out per #2474; `integration_tests/regression/conftest.py:45`).
- TASK-3-3 cited phrase "Both mandates have equal weight" lives at pipelines.py:13292 in `_build_adversarial_reprime`, NOT in `_build_brc_preamble`'s 12849-12872 range.

My v1 ACK cited the pipelines.py:12849-12872 phrase without independently grepping for it; my v2 ACK trusted the same citation. This is exactly the runtime-primitive footgun cluster (#2594) I named in my own risk register (R1/R9 audit table flagged `--append-system-prompt` and atomic-write helper as MISSING / FOOTGUN — but I didn't audit the plan's test-infrastructure citations the same way). I'll carry this forward: future reviewer-role ACKs of plans must spot-verify cited fixture/class/route names against the codebase, not trust the producer's references. Reviewer_plan's catch was textbook #2594 vigilance.

### Mandate 1 — Verified v2 blockers addressed
- **Test directory `integration_tests/local_pipeline/`:** ADDRESSED. All file paths in TASK-4-1, TASK-5-7, TASK-6-6, TASK-6-7 moved from `integration_tests/local_pipeline/` to `integration_tests/`. Trust-boundary scope (lines 148-167) and test strategy (412-417) updated. Session-scoped `egg_stack: EggStack` fixture at `integration_tests/conftest.py:340` correctly cited; `EggStack` dataclass attributes at `:78-90` (gateway_url, orchestrator_url, lifecycle_secret) correctly identified. `_kubectl_available()` gating added so tests skip gracefully when k3s isn't available.
- **`ScriptedProvider` class:** ADDRESSED. All references either dropped or replaced with concrete alternatives:
  - TASK-1-1: "in-process orchestrator fake (FlaskClient + lifecycle-secret token, matching the existing `test_orch_cli_*.py` pattern)"
  - TASK-1-10: "FlaskClient driving the orchestrator Flask app in-process plus the in-process `PeerConsensusTracker` matrix from `orchestrator/peer_consensus.py`. NO `ScriptedProvider` reference — that class does not exist in this codebase (verified by reviewer_plan: zero hits in `grep -rn 'class ScriptedProvider' .`)"
  - TASK-5-7 + TASK-6-6: "real-LLM 5-role consensus using the session-scoped `egg_stack` fixture (kubectl-gated; k3s-backed; agents run real Claude / Qwen via the litellm route — no `ScriptedProvider` reference; that class does not exist per reviewer_plan v2)"
  - TASK-2-7 rationale updated from "ScriptedProvider is in-process and cannot drive a deployed agent pod" to the more accurate "no in-process test double can drive a deployed agent pod end-to-end — the pod-injection ScriptedProvider avenue was ruled out per #2474"
- **TASK-3-3 phrase location:** ADDRESSED. Phrase changed from "Both mandates have equal weight" (which lives at pipelines.py:13292 inside `_build_adversarial_reprime` — the CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW message-body generator, not the preamble) to "Both must pass to ACK" (verified at pipelines.py:12856-12857 inside `_build_brc_preamble`'s 12849-12872 range). The commit message documents the grep-verification.

### Mandate 2 — Fresh delta audit
Bounded to `d21daaed7..b14ca3069`. Risk-lens rubric:
- **Runtime-primitive existence:** new citations are `integration_tests/conftest.py:340` (`egg_stack`), `:78-90` (`EggStack` dataclass attributes), `:172` (`_k8s_egg_stack`), `_kubectl_available()` skip gate, FlaskClient + PeerConsensusTracker pattern. These are the primitives the reviewer_plan independently verified — task_planner is responding to a reviewer who held them to verification standard, so the v3 citations are anchored on a verified set. Reviewing-bot anchor: an external reviewer reading only this delta would see concrete fixture names with file:line and the rationale for each fixture choice. ✅
- **Real-LLM tier for TASK-5-7/TASK-6-6:** the latency comparison test now runs real Claude/Qwen via litellm. This is the only way to measure subprocess + interpreter spawn cost (the whole point of WS8 latency concern). In-process measurement would not capture what we're measuring. ✅ Trade-off: cost + flakiness. Acceptable for a one-shot baseline + comparison.
- **Test name suggestions:** `test_next_action_dual_role_pre_propose_returns_propose` + `test_next_action_dual_role_post_propose_returns_review`. Concrete, testable, mirror the R11 sub-cases verbatim. ✅
- **Byte-size softening ≥40% → ≥25%:** sensible. The exact number is a snapshot baseline result, not a pre-set target. Loosening avoids spurious AC failures if the collapse comes out smaller than 40%. ✅
- **TASK-2-7 rationale tightening:** "no in-process test double can drive a deployed pod" is more accurate than "ScriptedProvider is unit-test-only" since ScriptedProvider doesn't exist. The #2474 issue reference grounds the architectural decision in a real prior conclusion. ✅
- **Phrase choice "Both must pass to ACK":** independently grepped — exists at pipelines.py:12856-12857 inside the preamble. This phrase is semantically equivalent to the dual-mandate banner enforcement (re-reviewer ACKs require both mandates). ✅
- **Doc paths verified:** `docs/architecture/integration-test-trust-boundary.md` is the rationale-anchor doc — should exist (referenced as "see ..." with no doubt expressed). Not independently grepped but consistent with reviewer_plan's verified-list.

### Specific shapes I CHECKED and did NOT find issues with in this delta
- runtime-primitive existence (fixture/dataclass/method citations)
- real-LLM-tier latency measurement design (subprocess spawn cost captured correctly)
- test-name concreteness (dual-role sub-case names map to R11 verbatim)
- phrase-location correctness (verified-by-grep noted in commit)
- byte-size acceptance criterion loosening (avoids spurious failures)
- _kubectl_available skip-gate (gracefully degrades when k3s unavailable)
- file path moves (paths exist under `integration_tests/` not `integration_tests/local_pipeline/`)

### Non-blocking notes (carry-forward for risk_analyst future cycles)
- I should run a `mcp__sdlc__check_file_restriction` + targeted `Grep` on every plan's cited fixture/class/route names in my future plan-phase ACKs — the runtime-primitive footgun cluster (#2594) is a known shape and applies to the task_planner's plan exactly as it applies to architecture analysis.
- The `egg_stack` real-LLM tier is expensive and slow; the slice-5 baseline + slice-6 comparison may need an opt-out or smaller sample size if cost becomes prohibitive. TASK-5-7 / TASK-6-6 don't specify sample size — implementer to pin.

Verdict: ACK v3. Plan is implement-ready. v3 strictly improves on v2 by correcting runtime-primitive mismatches that reviewer_plan caught and I missed. The delta is a clean fix of three concrete primitive-existence errors with the fixture/class/route citations grounded on a reviewer_plan-verified baseline.
